No vague information, no recycled content — just what works on the ground, checked against current RTA figures.\n\n## What Makes Public Transport in Dubai Worth Using\n### RTA — The Authority Behind It All\nThe Roads and Transport Authority (RTA) was established in 2005 and manages everything from the metro to marine transport — effectively running all of public transport in Dubai under one roof. Every mode of travel — metro, bus, or tram — feeds into the same NOL card account, and the RTA Journey Planner and the S'hail app are both official tools for trip planning. It operates two lines — the Red Line and the Green Line — across 55 stations spanning 90 kilometres. It's driverless, fully automated, and consistently on time. Understanding metro fares starts with the zone system — every trip is priced by how many fare zones you cross, not by distance alone.\n\n### Dubai Metro Fare Zones Table\nThe city is divided into seven fare zones, and your fare depends on how many you cross during a journey. The table below applies to Silver NOL card holders; Gold class fares are exactly double, and Blue (concession) card holders pay roughly half.\n\n| Zones Crossed | Silver NOL Fare (AED) | Silver NOL Fare (USD) | Example Journey |\n|-|-|-|-|\n| 1 Zone | AED 3.00 | ~USD 0.82 | Short hop within the same zone |\n| 2 Zones | AED 5.00 | ~USD 1.36 | Union to BurJuman |\n| 3 or More Zones (Max Fare) | AED 7.50 | ~USD 2.04 | Airport to Downtown Dubai; full Red Line journey |\n\n_Disclaimer: Metro fares are set by RTA and may change without prior notice._ _Confirm current fares on RTA's nol fares page before travel._\n\n## Dubai Bus: Routes, Fares and Rules\nThe bus network is massive — over 150 routes connect areas the metro doesn't reach. Transport in Dubai: Affordable RTA Metro & Taxi Guide](https://media.cdnbsd.com/articles/8178403300618963/banner/public-transport-in-dubai-affordable-rta-metro-taxi-guide.png)\n\nMost tourists land in Dubai expecting to spend a fortune on taxis. What they don't realise is that **public transport in Dubai** is one of the most efficient, affordable, and well-connected systems in the entire Middle East — and it covers almost every major destination in the city. If you're _**[planning a budget-friendly trip to Dubai](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/article/dubai-budget-trip \"planning a budget-friendly trip to Dubai\")**_, using it instead of taxis can save you hundreds of dirhams on even a short visit. Whether you're heading to the mall, the marina, or the museum, this network has a route for you.\n\n**Quick answer:** Public transport in Dubai runs on a single smartcard — the NOL card (from AED 25) — across metro, bus, tram, and water bus. Metro and bus fares range from AED 3 to AED 7.50 per trip depending on zones crossed, with a daily spending cap of AED 14. Buy a NOL card at any metro station, or at Terminal 1 or Terminal 3 on arrival.\n\nDubai's **Roads and Transport Authority (RTA)** runs the metro, buses, trams, abras, and ferries under one unified system. The metro network alone spans 90 kilometres of track across 55 stations on the Red and Green lines, backed by more than 150 bus routes across the emirate. On an average day in 2026, the metro carries upwards of 750,000 riders.\n\nThis guide covers everything about **public transport in Dubai** — fares in AED and USD, zone tables, **NOL card** costs for tourists, **Dubai tram** stops and hours, **Sharjah to Dubai bus fare**, **Ajman to Dubai bus fare**, airport connections, and the apps you actually need. No vague information, no recycled content — just what works on the ground, checked against current RTA figures.\n\n## What Makes Public Transport in Dubai Worth Using\n\nBefore we go into routes and fares, it helps to understand why this system is worth your attention — especially if you're visiting for the first time. It's fully integrated, meaning one card and one app handles everything.\n\n- All metro, bus, tram, and water transport runs under one NOL card — no separate tickets\n- Metro stations are air-conditioned end to end, including platform waiting areas\n- Buses are fully air-conditioned with real-time tracking via the S'hail app\n- Women and children get dedicated cabins on the metro at no extra charge\n- Metro, bus, and tram are entirely cashless — the one exception is the traditional abra, covered in the water transport section below\n\n### RTA — The Authority Behind It All\n\nThe Roads and Transport Authority (RTA) was established in 2005 and manages everything from the metro to marine transport — effectively running all of public transport in Dubai under one roof. Every mode of travel — metro, bus, or tram — feeds into the same NOL card account, and the RTA Journey Planner and the S'hail app are both official tools for trip planning. Fares and schedules change from time to time, so every figure in this guide has been checked against RTA's published fares — always confirm current numbers before you travel.\n\n## NOL Card for Tourists: Types, Cost and How It Works\n\nThe NOL card is your single access key to public transport in Dubai. Without one, you cannot board a bus or a tram. For the metro, paper Red Tickets exist but cost more per trip than a reloadable card. Every visitor planning more than a couple of rides should get a NOL card on arrival.\n\n### NOL Card Types and Prices\n\n| Card Type | Purchase Price (AED) | Purchase Price (USD) | Minimum Balance | Best For |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| Red Ticket (paper) | AED 2 (card only) | ~USD 0.55 | Load as needed | One-time or rare users |\n| Silver NOL Card | AED 25 (includes AED 19 credit) | ~USD 6.80 | AED 7.50 | Tourists, short-term visitors |\n| Gold NOL Card | AED 25 (includes AED 19 credit) | ~USD 6.80 | AED 7.50 | Gold-class metro travel (2x fare) |\n| Blue NOL Card | AED 70 (personalised, registered) | ~USD 19 | AED 7.50 | Students, seniors, People of Determination (~50% off fares) |\n\n_Disclaimer: Card prices and credit amounts are set by RTA and may change. Always verify current figures on RTA's nol fares page before purchasing._\n\nThe Silver NOL card is the right choice for most tourists. It works on the metro, buses, trams, and water buses. **It does not work on the Palm Monorail**, which is privately operated by Nakheel on separate ticketing — more on that in the tram section below. The Silver card is valid for 5 years, so it doesn't expire during a normal holiday, and it doubles as your metro card, bus card, and tram card in one — no separate tickets for any mode.\n\n### Getting Your NOL Card in 3 Steps\n\n1. Go to any metro station vending machine, the Terminal 1 or Terminal 3 metro station on arrival, or a Carrefour, ENOC, or EPPCO outlet.\n2. Choose Silver (standard) or Gold (premium cabin access) and pay AED 25 — this includes AED 19 of starting travel credit.\n3. Top up in AED 10+ increments at any station, through the RTA app, or at partner retailers whenever your balance runs low.\n\nLost card? You can recover the balance on a registered Silver or Gold card by visiting any RTA customer service centre with your Emirates ID or passport.\n\n### Tap In, Tap Out — The Rule Everyone Forgets\n\nThis is the single most common and costly mistake tourists make. On the metro, you must tap your NOL card both when entering the station gate and when exiting. If you forget to tap out, the system charges you the maximum fare for that journey — regardless of how far you actually travelled. On buses and the tram, tap once when boarding. No tap means no travel, and inspectors do check.\n\nThere is also a 30-minute free transfer window: tap out of the metro and into a connecting bus or tram within 30 minutes, and the transfer fare is discounted. This is one of the biggest advantages of using the network over paying for individual taxi rides.\n\n## What Are Dubai Metro and Tram Operating Hours?\n\nMetro and tram timings shifted when the UAE moved to a Monday–Friday working week in January 2022 — Sunday, not Friday, is now the shorter, later-starting day. These are RTA's current published hours:\n\n| Day | Metro Hours | Tram Hours |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Monday – Thursday | 5:00 AM – 12:00 Midnight | 6:00 AM – 1:00 AM (next day) |\n| Friday | 5:00 AM – 1:00 AM (next day) | 6:00 AM – 1:00 AM (next day) |\n| Saturday | 5:00 AM – 12:00 Midnight | 6:00 AM – 1:00 AM (next day) |\n| Sunday | 8:00 AM – 12:00 Midnight | 9:00 AM – 1:00 AM (next day) |\n\n_Disclaimer: Hours are extended during Ramadan and public holidays. Confirm live timings on the RTA app or S'hail before you travel._\n\n## Dubai Metro: Zones, Fares and Lines\n\nThe Dubai Metro is the backbone of public transport in Dubai. It operates two lines — the Red Line and the Green Line — across 55 stations spanning 90 kilometres. It's driverless, fully automated, and consistently on time. Understanding metro fares starts with the zone system — every trip is priced by how many fare zones you cross, not by distance alone.\n\n### Red Line and Green Line Overview\n\n- **Red Line:** Runs from Rashidiya (near Dubai International Airport) through Deira, Downtown Dubai, Business Bay, Dubai Marina, and out to Expo City / UAE Exchange. This is the line most tourists use.\n- **Green Line:** Covers Al Qusais, Deira, and Bur Dubai, connecting to the Red Line at Union and BurJuman — both interchange stations serving both lines.\n- **Gold Class:** The first cabin on every train is Gold Class — a quieter, less crowded option for Gold NOL card holders. Fares are double the standard rate.\n- **Women and Children's Cabin:** The last cabin is reserved for women and children at no extra cost.\n\n### Dubai Metro Fare Zones Table\n\nThe city is divided into seven fare zones, and your fare depends on how many you cross during a journey. The table below applies to Silver NOL card holders; Gold class fares are exactly double, and Blue (concession) card holders pay roughly half.\n\n| Zones Crossed | Silver NOL Fare (AED) | Silver NOL Fare (USD) | Example Journey |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| 1 Zone | AED 3.00 | ~USD 0.82 | Short hop within the same zone |\n| 2 Zones | AED 5.00 | ~USD 1.36 | Union to BurJuman |\n| 3 or More Zones (Max Fare) | AED 7.50 | ~USD 2.04 | Airport to Downtown Dubai; full Red Line journey |\n\n_Disclaimer: Metro fares are set by RTA and may change without prior notice. Confirm current fares on RTA's nol fares page before travel._\n\n### Metro from Dubai Airport\n\nGetting from the airport into Dubai by metro is straightforward — but the terminal matters. Terminal 1 and Terminal 3 each have their own dedicated Red Line station inside the terminal building, so you can walk straight from arrivals to a train. Terminal 2 has no metro station at all — see the \"Common Mistakes\" section below for how to get there instead.\n\nThe metro fare from either airport terminal to Downtown Dubai (Financial Centre station) is approximately AED 7.50 (~USD 2.04), since the journey crosses 3 or more zones. The same trip by taxi costs roughly AED 50–80 (~USD 14–22) depending on traffic. If you want to understand all your options from the airport, the _**[Dubai airport guide](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/article/dubai-airport-guide \"Dubai airport guide\")**_ covers transport, lounges, and terminal layouts in detail.\n\n## Dubai Bus: Routes, Fares and Rules\n\nThe bus network is massive — over 150 routes connect areas the metro doesn't reach. For residents living in areas like Al Quoz, Karama, Satwa, or Mirdif, the bus is the primary way to get around. For tourists, buses are useful for reaching beaches, souks, and residential neighbourhoods off the metro map. The entire network runs on the same NOL card as the metro and tram — all part of one connected public transport in Dubai system.\n\n### Dubai Bus Fare and How to Pay\n\nBus fares follow the same zone-based structure as the metro: AED 3 (~USD 0.82) for a single-zone journey, rising to AED 7.50 (~USD 2.04) for longer, cross-zone routes. There's a daily spending cap of AED 14 (~USD 3.81) on Silver and Blue NOL cards — once you hit it, further rides that day are free.\n\nYour NOL card is the only accepted form of payment on buses; there is no cash option on any RTA bus. If you board without a loaded card, you will not be able to travel, so buy or recharge before you board, not after.\n\n_Disclaimer: Bus fares and the daily cap are set by RTA and subject to revision. Always check the latest figures on RTA's nol fares page or via the S'hail app._\n\n### Bus Rules and Etiquette\n\nDubai buses have a clear set of rules that are enforced. Ignoring them leads to fines.\n\n- **No eating or drinking** on any RTA bus — fine of AED 100\n- **Women-only sections** exist on some intercity and longer routes — usually the front rows\n- **Queue at bus stops** — jumping the queue is considered disrespectful and may attract attention from inspectors\n- **Priority seating** near the front is reserved for elderly, pregnant women, and people of determination\n- **Noise rules** — phone calls on speaker and loud music are not acceptable on public buses\n\n## Dubai Trams: Everything You Need to Know\n\nThe tram is a dedicated surface rail line running through one of the most scenic parts of the city — and it's one of the most underused parts of public transport in Dubai among tourists. If you're staying in or near Dubai Marina, JBR, or Al Sufouh, the tram is your easiest and most pleasant way to get around without a car or taxi. Many visitors skip it entirely and pay AED 30–50 in taxis for distances the tram covers for AED 3.\n\nThe tram runs across all 11 stations along a 10.6-kilometre route from JBR in the north to Al Sufouh in the south. It connects to the Metro Red Line at two points — Sobha Realty station (via the Dubai Marina tram stop) and DMCC station (via the Jumeirah Lakes Towers tram stop) — and reaches the Palm Jumeirah tram stop, from which the Palm Monorail is a short walk across a footbridge. The tram itself runs on the same NOL card as the metro; the Palm Monorail does not (see below).\n\n### Tram Stations, in Order\n\n| # | Tram Station | Key Connection / Nearby Landmark |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| 1 | Jumeirah Beach Residence 1 | JBR The Walk, beach access |\n| 2 | Jumeirah Beach Residence 2 | The Beach mall, restaurants |\n| 3 | Jumeirah Lakes Towers (JLT) | **Metro Red Line interchange** — footbridge to DMCC station |\n| 4 | Dubai Marina Mall | Dubai Marina Mall, waterfront dining |\n| 5 | Dubai Marina | **Metro Red Line interchange** — footbridge to Sobha Realty station |\n| 6 | Marina Towers | Northern Marina residential towers, Marina Walk |\n| 7 | Mina Seyahi | Beachfront resorts, water sports |\n| 8 | Media City | Dubai Media City and Dubai Internet City offices |\n| 9 | Palm Jumeirah | **Palm Monorail footbridge** — separate ticket needed, access to Atlantis, Nakheel Mall |\n| 10 | Knowledge Village | Dubai Knowledge Park, universities |\n| 11 | Al Sufouh | Southern terminus |\n\n**Tram fares** follow the same NOL system as the metro. The entire tram route sits within a single fare zone, so a standard Silver NOL journey costs a flat AED 3.00 (~USD 0.82) — tap in, tap out, exactly as on the metro.\n\nThe **Palm Monorail**, run separately by Nakheel, is not part of the NOL network — you'll need to buy a monorail ticket online or at the station counter rather than tap your NOL card. The monorail reopened in mid-2026 after a maintenance closure; check current hours before you go.\n\nDuring Ramadan and public holidays (Eid, UAE National Day), tram timings are extended. Always check the RTA app or S'hail for current schedules. If you're staying nearby and want to make the most of the area, the _**[Dubai stopover guide](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/article/dubai-stopover-guide \"Dubai stopover guide\")**_ has a full day itinerary built around the Marina zone.\n\n_Disclaimer: Tram operating hours and fares are set by RTA and may be adjusted. Verify current information on RTA's website._\n\n## Water Transport in Dubai\n\nDubai has a functioning marine transport network that most tourists completely overlook — yet it's very much part of public transport in Dubai. Traditional wooden abras cross the Dubai Creek between Bur Dubai and Deira for just AED 1 (~USD 0.27) per ride — one of the cheapest and most iconic ways to experience old Dubai. Air-conditioned and petrol-powered abras on the same crossing cost a little more, around AED 2 (~USD 0.55).\n\nThe NOL card works on RTA water buses and the Dubai Ferry, but traditional and petrol abras are cash-only — they're the single exception to the network's otherwise cashless rule. For a detailed breakdown of all marine routes, fares, and the difference between abras, water buses, and ferries, the [_**Dubai water taxis guide**_](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/article/water-taxis-dubai \"Dubai water taxis guide\") covers everything you need to plan a full marine day in the city.\n\n## Best Apps for Public Transport in Dubai\n\nKnowing the routes is only half the job. These four apps make navigating the network significantly easier in practice.\n\n| App | What It Does | Best For |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| S'hail | Official RTA multi-modal trip planner — metro, bus, tram, water bus routes and real-time tracking | All public transport users |\n| RTA Dubai App | NOL balance check, recharge, taxi booking, vehicle registration | NOL card management |\n| Google Maps | Integrated public transit routes with walking directions and live bus times | Tourists and first-time visitors |\n| Careem | Ride-hailing for journeys where public transport doesn't reach | Late-night or off-route travel |\n\nThe S'hail app is particularly useful for checking real-time bus arrival times and confirming the exact fare for your route before you travel — it will show you the route, the fare, and the expected waiting time in one screen.\n\n## Metro, Bus, Tram or Taxi — Which Should You Use?\n\nNot sure which mode of public transport in Dubai suits your situation? This table breaks it down clearly.\n\n| Factor | Metro | Bus | Tram | Taxi / Careem |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| Cost | AED 3–7.50 | AED 3–7.50 | AED 3 (flat) | AED 12–80+ |\n| Speed | Fast, no traffic | Moderate | Slow but scenic | Depends on traffic |\n| Coverage | Major corridors | City-wide | Marina / JBR / Al Sufouh only | Door to door |\n| Payment | NOL card / Red Ticket | NOL card only | NOL card only | Cash / card / app |\n| Best For | Airport, malls, Downtown | Areas off the metro map | Marina / Palm access | Groups, luggage, night |\n\n_Disclaimer: Taxi fares are metered and subject to RTA rate revisions. Figures above are approximate ranges._\n\n## Final Word\n\nPublic transport in Dubai is one of the most advanced networks in the Arab world — and most visitors barely use it. A NOL card, the S'hail app, and basic knowledge of which metro line goes where will get you through almost every major destination in the city for a fraction of what taxis cost.\n\nWhether you're commuting from Sharjah on Route E303, riding in from Ajman on Route E400, taking the tram through the Marina, or just checking fares before you board, RTA's system genuinely works. Use it confidently. If you're still planning your overall trip and figuring out what to do and see, the _**[best places to visit in Dubai](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/article/best-places-to-visit-in-dubai \"best places to visit in Dubai\")**_ guide will help you map out your itinerary around the transport network — so every trip you take actually leads somewhere worth going.\n\nTags:public transport in dubaidubai transportationlocal transport in dubaidubai metrodubai bus routesdubai taxi faresapply dubai visit visastep by step\n\nWas this article helpful?\n\n![](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/images/blog/icon-thumb-up-a9f4be0f6e6aa589ba1f7eea00d658d5.svg)\nYes\n![](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/images/blog/icon-thumb-down-7b85b621af89d1e72b698b75f3d942fa.svg)\nNo\n\n\n![Priyanshu Raghav](https://media.cdnbsd.com/profile-photos/31/c3a0cf8a-0459-44ab-bcfe-cee3c5acb2ce.webp)\n\nAbout the authorPriyanshu Raghav\n\nPriyanshu is a travel and visa content specialist focused on visa requirements, travel procedures, and immigration information for international destinations. His work covers visa eligibility, documentation, application processes, entry requirements, transit rules, and practical travel guidance. He researches official updates and relevant industry information to create clear, accurate, and easy-to-understand content that helps travellers understand visa processes and plan their journeys with confidence.\n\n## Comments\n\n0\n\nNo comments yet — be the first to share your thoughts.\n\n\n### Leave a Comment\n\nYour email address will not be published. Required fields are marked \\*\n\nThanks! Your comment has been received and will appear after a quick review.\n\nSubmission failed.\n\nName\n\nEmail\n\nComment\n\nLeave this field empty\n\n![](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/images/blog/icon-post-send-d53a56193a83ca99e72e32eb0a3a0f88.svg)\nPost Comment\n\n\nThis site is protected by reCAPTCHA and the Google\n[Privacy Policy](https://policies.google.com/privacy) and\n[Terms of Service](https://policies.google.com/terms) apply.\n\n\n## Frequently asked questions\n\nHow much does a NOL card cost for tourists in Dubai?![](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/images/blog/icon-faq-plus-b9f60a9dd8b9c0ecc46a0660d5e426cf.svg)\n\nThe Silver NOL card — which is the recommended dubai nol card for tourists — costs AED 25 (~USD 6.80), which includes AED 19 in pre-loaded travel credit. It works on metro, buses, trams, and water buses and acts as your dubai bus card for all modes of dubai public transport.\n\nWhat is the dubai bus fare from Sharjah to Dubai?![](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/images/blog/icon-faq-plus-b9f60a9dd8b9c0ecc46a0660d5e426cf.svg)\n\nThe sharjah to dubai bus fare on Route E303 is AED 5.00 (~USD 1.36) using a NOL card. The bus runs between Al Jubail Bus Station in Sharjah and Union Metro Station in Dubai, connecting you directly to dubai public transit. Payment is by NOL card only — no cash accepted on any intercity route.\n\nCan I use the same NOL card on the metro, bus, and tram?![](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/images/blog/icon-faq-plus-b9f60a9dd8b9c0ecc46a0660d5e426cf.svg)\n\nYes. One NOL card works across all RTA transport — metro, buses, trams, water buses, and the Palm Monorail. There is no need for separate tickets for each mode of transport. The daily spending cap is AED 20 regardless of how many modes you use.\n\nWhat is the fare from Dubai Airport to Downtown by metro?![](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/images/blog/icon-faq-plus-b9f60a9dd8b9c0ecc46a0660d5e426cf.svg)\n\nThe metro fare from Airport Terminal 3 to Financial Centre station (Downtown area) is approximately AED 7.50 (~USD 2.04) on a Silver NOL card. The journey takes roughly 25–30 minutes with no traffic delays, making it significantly cheaper than a taxi for solo travellers.\n\nDoes the Dubai tram connect to the Palm Jumeirah?![](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/images/blog/icon-faq-plus-b9f60a9dd8b9c0ecc46a0660d5e426cf.svg)\n\nYes. The Dubai tram connects to the Palm Monorail at Palm Jumeirah station. From there, the monorail takes you along the Palm trunk to Atlantis The Palm. The full tram journey from Dubai Marina Mall to the Palm station takes under 15 minutes.\n\nAre there women-only areas on Dubai public transport?![](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/images/blog/icon-faq-plus-b9f60a9dd8b9c0ecc46a0660d5e426cf.svg)\n\nYes. The metro has a dedicated Women and Children's cabin at the front of every train at no additional cost. Some bus routes also have women-only seating sections. These are clearly marked. Male passengers found in these sections may face a fine.\n\nWhat happens if I forget to tap out on the Dubai metro?![](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/images/blog/icon-faq-plus-b9f60a9dd8b9c0ecc46a0660d5e426cf.svg)\n\nIf you forget to tap out, the system automatically deducts the maximum possible fare for your journey from your NOL card balance — regardless of how short your actual trip was. Always tap both at the entry and exit gates to pay the correct fare.\n\n## Related Articles\n\n[View all Articles\\\\\n![](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/images/blog/icon-view-all-arrow-0d0d48a6671f5a1ea5eaae8929a0711c.svg)](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/article)\n\n![How to Apply Dubai Prearranged Visit Visa: Complete Guide](https://media.cdnbsd.com/articles/4390143883402312/post/how-to-apply-dubai-prearranged-visit-visa-complete-guide.png)Tourist Visa![](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/images/blog/card-notch-stroke-5502755da0f7b5a1ff52dcc1544e00dd.svg)![](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/images/blog/card-notch-312ff4613f1dfae5f896bac8091ed678.svg)[![](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/images/blog/icon-whatsapp-share-dda73dacb367112e49d9ece467192156.svg)](https://wa.me/?text=How%20to%20Apply%20Dubai%20Prearranged%20Visit%20Visa:%20Complete%20Guide%20%E2%80%94%20https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/article/dubai-prearranged-visa)\n\n![](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/images/blog/icon-author-e26ad1aa01b483eb7ab5c380029e4b13.svg)Priyanshu Raghav\n\n### [How to Apply Dubai Prearranged Visit Visa: Complete Guide](https://dubaivisitsvisa.com/article/dubai-prearranged-visa)\n\nApply for a Dubai prearranged visit visa with confidence. PTA operates an extended bus network of 119 internal lines, including 35 lines linking to metro stations, 12 intercity lines to transport passengers to other emirates, 62 internal lines and 8 fast lines. PTA operates an extended bus network of 119 internal lines, including 35 lines linking to metro stations, 12 intercity lines to transport passengers to other emirates, 62 internal lines and 8 fast lines. The network covers 82% of the urban areas in Dubai, and transports about 369,248 passengers per day.\n\n- \"Click here to scroll to Our buses section\")\n\n## Facilities\n\nFacilities include facilities for public transport services, 17 fully equipped stations for buses and 2185 bus stands, including 554 air-conditioned waiting areas for passengers, and most bus stops include all information regarding bus timings and schedule dates.\n\n![Limousine taxi](https://www.rta.ae/wps/wcm/connect/rta/3248abb9-eb24-48d1-8af9-d851e57a11e2/FE_dubai_bus_icon-1.png?MOD=AJPERES&CACHEID=ROOTWORKSPACE.Z18_N004G041LOBR60AUHP2NT32000-3248abb9-eb24-48d1-8af9-d851e57a11e2-p8EB-31)\n\n### Air conditioned\n\nAir conditioner always working on all buses\n\n![Limousine taxi](https://www.rta.ae/wps/wcm/connect/rta/0729e8ec-1808-4dd8-aed7-42cbe1f8a807/FE_dubai_bus_icon-9.png?MOD=AJPERES&CACHEID=ROOTWORKSPACE.Z18_N004G041LOBR60AUHP2NT32000-0729e8ec-1808-4dd8-aed7-42cbe1f8a807-oNIqk97)\n\n### Cafeteria & vending machines\n\nAvailable and installed in all main bus stops\n\n![Limousine taxi](https://www.rta.ae/wps/wcm/connect/rta/44315249-6d38-4973-9b3d-f33cc055bce8/E-Card+Machines.png?MOD=AJPERES&CACHEID=ROOTWORKSPACE.Z18_N004G041LOBR60AUHP2NT32000-44315249-6d38-4973-9b3d-f33cc055bce8-lRaPIag)\n\n### E-Card Machines\n\nTag in and out, with convenience\n\n![Limousine taxi](https://www.rta.ae/wps/wcm/connect/rta/f25d1bc7-3982-4bf2-a156-760f74739880/FE_dubai_bus_icon-4-new.png?MOD=AJPERES&CACHEID=ROOTWORKSPACE.Z18_N004G041LOBR60AUHP2NT32000-f25d1bc7-3982-4bf2-a156-760f74739880-oqpDwXD)\n\n### Accessibility\n\nConvenience for the commuters has always been a prime focus of the Dubai bus service, and in that regard, below are the accessibility services that are available for the bus users:\n\n![Limousine taxi](https://www.rta.ae/wps/wcm/connect/rta/d1f9fa56-b1b8-47d8-980e-d109c2ca1dcd/Accessibility_dubai_bus_icon-5.png?MOD=AJPERES&CACHEID=ROOTWORKSPACE.Z18_N004G041LOBR60AUHP2NT32000-d1f9fa56-b1b8-47d8-980e-d109c2ca1dcd-lRaPE88)\n\n### Informative Displays\n\nInformation displayed on screens at all Bus stops.\n\n![Limousine taxi](https://www.rta.ae/wps/wcm/connect/rta/8edf2bec-f4bc-49fd-abea-079dda2f904c/Platform+Alerts.png?MOD=AJPERES&CACHEID=ROOTWORKSPACE.Z18_N004G041LOBR60AUHP2NT32000-8edf2bec-f4bc-49fd-abea-079dda2f904c-lRaPCJ7)\n\n### Platform Alerts\n\nPlatform alerts such as audible beeps when doors open and close and corresponding flashing light signals.\n\n![Limousine taxi](https://www.rta.ae/wps/wcm/connect/rta/f9cdc7bd-58b1-4842-abf6-4734a741b521/Tactile+Guidance+Path.png?MOD=AJPERES&CACHEID=ROOTWORKSPACE.Z18_N004G041LOBR60AUHP2NT32000-f9cdc7bd-58b1-4842-abf6-4734a741b521-orKscvL)\n\n### Tactile Guidance Path\n\nContrasting tactile guidance path for those with difficulty in seeing\n\n![Limousine taxi](https://www.rta.ae/wps/wcm/connect/rta/5c2919ef-66a7-4af2-b5f7-94a7402dfa9c/FE_dubai_bus_icon-4-new.png?MOD=AJPERES&CACHEID=ROOTWORKSPACE.Z18_N004G041LOBR60AUHP2NT32000-5c2919ef-66a7-4af2-b5f7-94a7402dfa9c-mXfIu6q)\n\n### Fines](https://www.rta.ae/wps/portal/rta/ae/public-transport/dubaibus/!ut/p/z1/jZDBboJAFEW_hi3vzUBxcDdUMzgqSoMRZ9NgQ0cSYAzQ8vsSjIsmlvbu3ss5yc0FBSmoOvsudNYVps7K4T4p730tXKQBo1tkS4787cXzEzGjrwsPjiMQ7QkLAyQbdJIA-SFOZOzLVUgcUP_x8Zdw_NtXIxIhugJdsmHb3RI5keFO0JgK6T6AiYojMNFBgtKlOd_34PXZYRpUk3_mTd7YX83wvnTdtZ1baGHf97Y2Rpe5_WEqC58pF9N2kP4k4VodUiz21ZG1_AZJ57ru/#Rules \"Click here to see Dubai Bus Rules & Fines\")\n\n- While waiting at the bus stop, stay out of the bus stopping area. Please signal the bus to stop.\n- Stay away from the bus doors till the driver opens them to allow you to board.\n- Board the bus through the front door and alight from the rear. Ladies and children may alight from the front door.\n- Do not engage in conversation or arguments with the bus driver.\n- Do not play or operate any musical instrument or sound reproducing equipment to the annoyance of  other passengers or the bus driver.\n- Do not beg or solicit any favours while on the bus.\n- If you wish to alight at an authorized bus stop, press the stop bells.\n- Do not attempt to leave the bus until the bus comes to a complete halt.\n\n- Bulky luggage, hazardous/obnoxious materials and animals are not allowed inside the bus.\n- Do not place your luggage in the gangway.\n\nLearn about the fines related to the following categories in addition to some policies.\n\n- [Fare](https://www.rta.ae/wps/portal/rta/ae/public-transport/dubaibus/!ut/p/z1/jZDBboJAFEW_hi3vzUBxcDdUMzgqSoMRZ9NgQ0cSYAzQ8vsSjIsmlvbu3ss5yc0FBSmoOvsudNYVps7K4T4p730tXKQBo1tkS4787cXzEzGjrwsPjiMQ7QkLAyQbdJIA-SFOZOzLVUgcUP_x8Zdw_NtXIxIhugJdsmHb3RI5keFO0JgK6T6AiYojMNFBgtKlOd_34PXZYRpUk3_mTd7YX83wvnTdtZ1baGHf97Y2Rpe5_WEqC58pF9N2kP4k4VodUiz21ZG1_AZJ57ru/#01)\n- AED 200\n\n- Using public transport modes, facilities and services or entering / existing fare zones without paying the due fare.\n- Failure to present a nol card upon request.\n- Using a card designated for others\n- Using an expired card.\n- Using an invalid card.\n- Selling nol cards without prior permit from the RTA\n\n##### AED 500\n\n- Using a fake card.\n\n##### AED 200\n\n- Selling or promoting, in any way goods and commodities inside public transports and public transport facilities\n- Failure to comply with the instruction of the inspector or authorized personnel of the authority or obstructing the performance of their duties.\n- Using public transports, public transport facilities and services, contrary to the authority's instructions posted on signboards.\n\n##### AED 100\n\n- Entry into restricted areas inside public transports, public transport facilities and services in violation of posted warning signs and boards\n- Accessing or sitting in areas designated for specific categories\n\n##### AED 100 per day up to 1000\n\n- Parking vehicles in areas designated for public transport users for a period exceeding the permitted period\n\n##### AED 2000\n\n- Damaging, vandalizing or destroying equipment’s on public transports, public transport facilities and services.\n\n##### AED 100\n\n- Causing a disturbance or inconvenience in any way to users of public transports, public transport facilities and services.\n- Misusing lifts or escalators.\n\n##### AED 100\n\n- Standing or sitting in non-passenger areas inside public transports, public transport facilities and services.\n- Putting feet on seats\n\n##### AED 200\n\n- Spitting, littering, or performing any act that would compromise the cleanness of public transports, public transport facilities and services.\n- Smoking inside public transports, public transport facilities and services.\n\n##### AED 100\n\n- Eating and Drinking in the areas where eating and drinking are not allowed\n\n##### AED 1000\n\n- Carrying hazardous items, including weapons, sharp tools or inflammable materials inside public transports, public transport facilities and services.\n\n##### AED 500\n\n- Carrying Alcoholic beverages inside public transports, public transport facilities and services.\n\n##### AED 100\n\n- Boarding the public transports, public transport facilities and services by climbing or jumping. Opening the doors or attempting to access or leave public transport while it is moving between stations and stops Bringing animals into public transports, public transport facilities and services, except guide dogs for blind people Carrying or using materials or equipment that may cause inconvenience to users of public transports, public transport facilities and services or endangering their safety\n\n##### AED 2000\n\n- Using any security or safety device or tool, including emergency exits where it is not necessary\n\n##### AED 300\n\n- Sleeping in passenger shelters or any place where sleeping is prohibited\n\n##### AED 200\n\n- Causing any distraction or obstruction to driver of public transport facilities or Services while he is driving\n\n##### Unaccompanied Minors Policy:\n\n- Below 8 years: It is not allowed to use public transport without the company of adults./li>\n- Between 8 to 11 years: a permit to be issued by their parents except for inter-city busses.\n- From 12 years and above: It is permitted to travel on public transportation without the company of adults.\n\n## Our buses\n\n### Double-decker\n\nnumber of buses 170\n\n![Double decker bus         "title": "sayer card registration - Sharjah - Mowasalat",
        "description": "# sayer card registration\n## sayer cards availability\nSayer Cards are available on-board Sharjah Public Transport Buses by paying the driver.\n\n## sales options\nSayer Cards can be purchased and recharged in Sharjah Public Transport buses by asking the driver.\n\n#### sayer discount card\n![](https://mowasalat.ae/build/assets/discount-card-c61c6afd.png)\n\nPassengers can purchase new SAYER Discount Cards from on-board Sharjah Public Transport buses\n\nAED 50\n\nwill have a pre-charged amount of AED 45\n\nAED 95\n\nwill have a pre-charged amount of AED 90\n\nNote: An initial Sayer Card purchase fee of AED 5 inclusive of 5% VAT which covers the cost of the card\n\n#### sayer subscription card\n![](https://mowasalat.ae/build/assets/subscription-card-93abbd84.png)\n\nPassengers can purchase new SAYER Subscription Cards from on-board Sharjah Public Transport buses\n\nAED 250.00\n\nUnlimited Travel Valid for 30 Days\n\nNote: An initial Sayer Card purchase fee of AED 5 inclusive of 5% VAT which covers the cost of the card\n\n## How to Top-up Sayer Cards in the Bus?\n1. Board any Sharjah Public Transport bus and ask the driver for SAYER Discount Card or SAYER Subscription Card top-up\n2. Inform the amount to the driver to top-up.\n3. Take the SAYER Card together with the purchase receipt.\n4. The purchase receipt will show the previous balance plus the amount added to your SAYER Card.\n\n## cash & sayer ticket price\ncash ticket:\n\nAED 9.00\n\n#### sayer discount card\n![](https://mowasalat.ae/build/assets/discount-card-c61c6afd.png)\n\nsayer ticket\n\nAED 7.00\n\ncard recharge options:\n\nAED 45 & AED 90\n\ncard price:\n\nOne time payment of AED 5.00 inclusive of 5% VAT for the new card cost\n\nrechargable:\n\nYes\n\nValidity:\n\n5 years\n\n#### sayer subscription card\n![](https://mowasalat.ae/build/assets/subscription-card-93abbd84.png)\n\nsayer subscription ticket\n\nUnlimited Travel with Sayer Subscription Ticket\n\nMonthly recharge:\n\nAED 250.00\n\nCard Price:\n\nOne time payment of AED 5.00 inclusive of 5% VAT for the new card cost\n\nRechargeable:\n\nYes\n\nValidity:\n\n5 years\n\n## Where can I buy and recharge my Sayer card?\nYou can buy or recharge your Sayer card at the nearest bus station (Al Jubail Bus Station), Sayer sales points, or Sayer vending machines inside the transport buses.",
        "position": 3,
        "markdown": "# sayer card registration\n\nSayer card is a way to facilitate your travel around Sharjah. It enables the passengers to pay tickets electronically on Sharjah Public Transport buses. This technology can save your precious time whenever you travel by bus.\n\n[register your sayer card now](https://mowasalat.ae/sayer/registration)\n\n![](https://mowasalat.ae/build/assets/sayer-17c36cda.png)\n\n## Why Sayer card is a smarter way to travel\n\n- It's convenient\n- Cash-free method of payment for travel\n- Access buses easily by passing the card into the card reader\n\nUsing Your SAYER Card for Sharjah Public Transport: Ticketing and Guidelines\n\n- Present your SAYER card on a Ticket machine in Sharjah Public Transport bus.\n- The trip amount will be deducted from your SAYER Card.\n- Please keep the SAYER ticket receipt till the end of journey for the inspection purpose.\n- SAYER Ticket Receipt will have details of amount charged and balance amount available in your SAYER Card\n- The time interval for issuing the second ticket is 15 minutes\n\n## sayer cards availability\n\nSayer Cards are available on-board Sharjah Public Transport Buses by paying the driver.\n\n## sales options\n\nSayer Cards can be purchased and recharged in Sharjah Public Transport buses by asking the driver.\n\n#### sayer discount card\n\n![](https://mowasalat.ae/build/assets/discount-card-c61c6afd.png)\n\nPassengers can purchase new SAYER Discount Cards from on-board Sharjah Public Transport buses\n\nAED 50\n\nwill have a pre-charged amount of AED 45\n\nAED 95\n\nwill have a pre-charged amount of AED 90\n\nNote: An initial Sayer Card purchase fee of AED 5 inclusive of 5% VAT which covers the cost of the card\n\n#### sayer subscription card\n\n![](https://mowasalat.ae/build/assets/subscription-card-93abbd84.png)\n\nPassengers can purchase new SAYER Subscription Cards from on-board Sharjah Public Transport buses\n\n\nAED 250.00\n\nUnlimited Travel Valid for 30 Days\n\nNote: An initial Sayer Card purchase fee of AED 5 inclusive of 5% VAT which covers the cost of the card\n\n## How to Top-up Sayer Cards in the Bus?\n\n1. Board any Sharjah Public Transport bus and ask the driver for SAYER Discount Card or SAYER Subscription Card top-up\n2. Inform the amount to the driver to top-up.\n3. Take the SAYER Card together with the purchase receipt.\n4. The purchase receipt will show the previous balance plus the amount added to your SAYER Card.\n\n## sayer card registration\n\nBenefits of Registering SAYER Cards\n\nThe registered SAYER Cards can be hot-listed, an important safeguard should it be lost, stolen or damaged. Hot-listing disables the SAYER Cards from further use and enables the value on the lost, stolen or damaged SAYER Cards to be transferred to a replacement SAYER Cards.\n\nOnce a SAYER Card has been registered to a person, it cannot be re-registered in another persons name.\n\n\nNote: Unregistered SAYER Cards will not be eligible for replacement and balance transfer.\n\n## cash & sayer ticket price\n\ncash ticket:\n\nAED 9.00\n\n#### sayer discount card\n\n![](https://mowasalat.ae/build/assets/discount-card-c61c6afd.png)\n\nsayer ticket\n\nAED 7.00\n\ncard recharge options:\n\nAED 45 & AED 90\n\ncard price:\n\nOne time payment of AED 5.00 inclusive of 5% VAT for the new card cost\n\n\nrechargable:\n\nYes\n\nValidity:\n\n5 years\n\n#### sayer subscription card\n\n![](https://mowasalat.ae/build/assets/subscription-card-93abbd84.png)\n\nsayer subscription ticket\n\nUnlimited Travel with Sayer Subscription Ticket\n\nMonthly recharge:\n\nAED 250.00\n\nCard Price:\n\nOne time payment of AED 5.00 inclusive of 5% VAT for the new card cost\n\n\nRechargeable:\n\nYes\n\nValidity:\n\n5 years\n\n## FAQ\n\n## What is the difference between SAYER cards and normal tickets?\n\nThe difference between SAYER Cards and normal tickets is that SAYER Cards are reloadable/rechargeable, easy access on the bus, saves your precious time, while the paper ticket trip has to be paid to the bus driver in cash which is cheaper than a Cash ticket.\n\n## How can I check the remaining balance and validity of my SAYER cards?\n\nThe balance and validity of your SAYER Cards are shown in your SAYER ticket issued by the bus driver every time you travel with Sharjah Public Transport Buses.\n\n## What can I do if SAYER card is lost or stolen?\n\nImmediately call the customer service hotline at 600522282 by reporting your card serial number and our agent will disable the card within 48 hours. Only registered Sayer Cards can be hot-listed.\n\n## How should I look after my SAYER card?\n\nKeep your card in its wallet, separate it from other credit/debit cards and don't bend the card.\n\n## Where can I buy and recharge my Sayer card?\n\nYou can buy or recharge your Sayer card at the nearest bus station (Al Jubail Bus Station), Sayer sales points, or Sayer vending machines inside the transport buses.\n\n## What is the benefit of registering my Sayer card?\n\nRegistering your Sayer card is essential to protect your card in case it is lost or damaged. The registration process allows you to deactivate your card if it is stolen, for example, and enables you to transfer the available balance to another card.",

      {
        "url": "https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/explore/activities/public-transport-in-dubai-ta/1010730?utm_id=ZLlagP1D",
        "title": "Public Transport in Dubai: The Complete Guide for Visitors",
        "description": "# Public Transport in Dubai: The Complete Guide for Visitors\n![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/blog/2020/01/traveloka-godwit-1.png)\n\nTraveloka Team\n\n4 Sep 2026 - 8 min read\n\n![](https://d1785e74lyxkqq.cloudfront.net/_next/static/v4.6.0/6/6904cd2e74ef73120833cff12185a320.svg)\n\n![](https://d1785e74lyxkqq.cloudfront.net/_next/static/v4.6.0/4/41064b24b4d41c7cd43a8f8006401b94.svg)\n\n![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/dam/i/01m19ky5s0dmwk5cy17dskszpp.png?tr=q-70,c-at_max,w-500,h-250,dpr-2)\n\nDubai has one of the most advanced public transport networks in the Middle East, and for visitors, it is genuinely one of the easiest cities in the region to navigate without a private car. From a fully automated metro to air-conditioned bus shelters and traditional water crossings, the Roads and Transport Authority ( _Hay'at al-Turuq wal-Muwasilat_), known as the RTA, runs a connected system that covers the city's major attractions, business districts, and the airport.\n\nUnderstanding how it all fits together before you arrive saves both time and money.\n\n## The Dubai Metro\nThe Dubai Metro is the backbone of the city's public transport system. It is fully automated, entirely air-conditioned, and runs on two interlinking lines covering 50 stations across the city. Trains operate every 2 to 4 minutes during peak hours and every 5 to 7 minutes during off-peak times, making waits short and journeys predictable.\n\nPlanning your trip to Dubai starts with knowing which line takes you where. Whether you are heading to the beach, the _souk_, or a skyscraper observation deck, there is likely a Metro station within walking distance of your destination.\n\n## Discover Flights With Traveloka\nTue, 24 Nov 2026\n\n![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/image/imageResource/2023/10/23/1698049654067-f06979c2b4fb31ea86fc9a8970c0da7b.png?tr=q-75,w-50)\n\nSaudia\n\nRiyadh (RUH) to Dubai (DXB)\n\nStart from USD 54.78\n\nBook Now\n\nWed, 25 Nov 2026\n\n![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/image/imageResource/2024/09/25/1727240468931-5d603f94d079b16372f8a80844f7e113.png?tr=q-75,w-50)\n\nCebu Pacific\n\nManila (MNL) to Dubai (DXB)\n\nStart from USD 191.33\n\nBook Now\n\nSun, 8 Nov 2026\n\n![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/image/imageResource/2025/12/03/1764758836364-3f7a66d4c168e9177372d70179c19d06.png?tr=q-75,w-50)\n\nAir India\n\nSingapore (SIN) to Dubai (DXB)\n\nStart from USD 205.44\n\nBook Now\n\n### Red Line\nThe Red Line is the longer of the two lines, running from Centrepoint Station in Rashidiya in the east all the way south to Expo City Dubai. It passes through the most visited areas of the city, including Union interchange station, BurJuman, the World Trade Centre, Financial Centre, Mall of the Emirates, Dubai Marina, and Jumeirah Lake Towers. If you are staying along Sheikh Zayed Road or in the Marina area, the Red Line will be your most-used route.\n\n## Getting to and from Dubai Airport by Metro\nDubai International Airport (DXB) is directly connected to the Metro via the Red Line, making it one of the most airport-accessible cities in the world. Two stations serve the airport: Airport Terminal 1 Station and Airport Terminal 3 Station, both on the Red Line. Terminal 1 serves flydubai and other airlines, while Terminal 3 is the main hub for Emirates and several other carriers.\n\nFrom Airport Terminal 3, you can ride the Red Line directly to Union Station (the main interchange with the Green Line), to the Dubai Mall area, to Mall of the Emirates, and all the way to Dubai Marina — all without changing trains. Nol Card vending machines are available inside both airport terminal stations, so you can purchase your card immediately after clearing arrivals.\n\nSearching for the right hotel before you fly makes the rest of the trip easier. Here are some great places to stay across Dubai, from budget-friendly options to luxury resorts.\n\n![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/image/imageResource/2020/10/27/1603791006056-0fc93a0066574a5de96895781f042eb5.png?tr=q-75,w-16,h-16,dpr-1.5)\n\nZa'abeel First\n\n![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/apr-asset/TzEv3ZUmG4-4Dz22hvmO9NUDzw1DGCIdWl4oPtKumOg=/lodging/97000000/96200000/96193600/96193558/dc91cfcd_z.jpg?tr=q-70,h-400,w-500)\n\nOne&Only One Za'abeel\n\n![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/image/imageResource/2019/08/26/1566837522839-f99f6d79d7e2647d89a58d4f785df9eb.png?tr=q-75,w-16,h-16,dpr-1.5)![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/image/imageResource/2019/08/26/1566837522839-f99f6d79d7e2647d89a58d4f785df9eb.png?tr=q-75,w-16,h-16,dpr-1.5)![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/image/imageResource/2019/08/26/1566837522839-f99f6d79d7e2647d89a58d4f785df9eb.png?tr=q-75,w-16,h-16,dpr-1.5)![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/image/imageResource/2019/08/26/1566837522839-f99f6d79d7e2647d89a58d4f785df9eb.png?tr=q-75,w-16,h-16,dpr-1.5)![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/image/imageResource/2019/08/26/1566837522839-f99f6d79d7e2647d89a58d4f785df9eb.png?tr=q-75,w-16,h-16,dpr-1.5)\n\n•\n\n![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/image/imageResource/2019/07/18/1563438542314-06efe4f1d97438b8448858c64510d098.png)\n\n9.4/10\n\nUSD 128.41\n\n## RTA Buses\nThe RTA bus network is the most extensive part of Dubai's public transport system, with over 1,500 buses operating across more than 100 routes. The network reaches 82% of the city's urban area, making it the only public transport option for neighbourhoods not served by the Metro or tram. Buses operate from around 4:00 AM to 1:00 AM, with some routes running 24 hours.\n\nBus fares use the same Nol Card and the same zone-based pricing as the Metro: AED 3 for one zone, AED 5 for two zones, and AED 7.50 for three or more zones. The 30-minute free transfer window from the Metro applies to bus journeys too, so connecting trips from the Metro to a bus cost nothing if timed correctly.\n\nOne distinctive feature of Dubai's bus system is its air-conditioned bus shelters. Given summer temperatures that regularly exceed 40°C, the RTA has installed air-conditioned waiting areas at hundreds of stops across the city, complete with real-time digital arrival boards. These shelters are fully accessible and make waiting for a bus a genuinely comfortable experience even in the hottest months.\n\n## Top Tips for Using Public Transport in Dubai\n### Check the Women and Children's Cabin\nThe Women and Children's cabin on the Metro is located at a specific end of the train, clearly marked by signage on the platform and on the train doors. Female travellers are welcome to use either Silver Class or the Women and Children's cabin — the dedicated cabin tends to be quieter and less crowded during peak hours.\n\n### Use the S'hail App for Journey Planning\nThe RTA's official journey planning app consolidates real-time timetables, route maps, and Nol Card top-up options for the Metro, bus, tram, and water transport into a single interface.\n\n## Book Your Trip with Traveloka\nWhether you are flying into Dubai, searching for the right hotel in the Marina or Downtown, or looking for [activities](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/activities?funnel_source=content_article&funnel_id=WP_1010730) to fill your itinerary, everything is in one place.\n\nBook [flights](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/flight?funnel_source=content_article&funnel_id=WP_1010730) and compare hundreds of options to find the best fare for your Dubai trip. Once you land, browse a wide range of [hotels](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/hotel?funnel_source=content_article&funnel_id=WP_1010730) — from design-forward city properties on Sheikh Zayed Road to beachfront resorts on Palm Jumeirah — and filter by price, location, and rating to find the right fit. For getting to and from the airport with ease, [airport transfer](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/airport-transfer?funnel_source=content_article&funnel_id=WP_1010730) options are bookable in advance so you arrive without the stress of arranging transport on arrival.\n\nDownload the Traveloka app from the Google Play Store or Apple App Store to manage your entire Dubai journey in one place.",
        "position": 4,
        "markdown": "![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)\n\n[![](https://d1785e74lyxkqq.cloudfront.net/_next/static/v4.6.0/9/97f3e7a54e9c6987283b78e016664776.svg)](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en)\n\nUSD \\| EN\n\n[![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/image/imageResource/2024/10/13/1728842433632-ac9d7312913263f7f64ac5d028ae0f0d.png?tr=h-20,q-75,w-20)\\\\\n\\\\\nDeals](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/promotion)\n\n[Get App](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/download-app)\n\n[Partnership](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/partnership)\n\nSupport\n\n[Bookings](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/retrieve)\n\nLog In\n\nRegister\n\n[Hotels](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/hotel) [Flights](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/flight) [Airport Transfer](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/airport-transfer) [Car Rental](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/car-rental) [Things to Do](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/activities) [Cruises](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/p/cruise)\n\nMore\n\n[traveloka \\\\\n\\\\\nExplore](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/explore)\n\n[/ activities](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/explore/activities)\n\n# Public Transport in Dubai: The Complete Guide for Visitors\n\n![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/blog/2020/01/traveloka-godwit-1.png)\n\nTraveloka Team\n\n4 Sep 2026 - 8 min read\n\n![](https://d1785e74lyxkqq.cloudfront.net/_next/static/v4.6.0/6/6904cd2e74ef73120833cff12185a320.svg)\n\n![](https://d1785e74lyxkqq.cloudfront.net/_next/static/v4.6.0/4/41064b24b4d41c7cd43a8f8006401b94.svg)\n\n![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/dam/i/01m19ky5s0dmwk5cy17dskszpp.png?tr=q-70,c-at_max,w-500,h-250,dpr-2)\n\nDubai has one of the most advanced public transport networks in the Middle East, and for visitors, it is genuinely one of the easiest cities in the region to navigate without a private car. From a fully automated metro to air-conditioned bus shelters and traditional water crossings, the Roads and Transport Authority ( _Hay'at al-Turuq wal-Muwasilat_), known as the RTA, runs a connected system that covers the city's major attractions, business districts, and the airport.\n\nUnderstanding how it all fits together before you arrive saves both time and money. This guide covers every mode of public transport in Dubai — fares, routes, tips, and the one card you need to use them all.\n\n## The Nol Card: Your Key to All Public Transport in Dubai\n\nBefore you board anything, you need a Nol Card. This stored-value smart card is accepted on every mode of RTA public transport in Dubai — the Metro, buses, the Dubai Tram, and water bus services. There is no option to pay with cash on the Metro, so picking up a Nol Card on arrival is the first thing to do.\n\nNol Cards are available from vending machines and ticket offices at any Dubai Metro station, including the two airport stations. The card itself costs between AED 2 and AED 25 depending on the type you choose.\n\n### Silver Nol Card\n\nThe Silver Nol Card is the standard option for most visitors. It costs AED 25, which includes AED 19 in pre-loaded travel credit, leaving AED 6 as the card deposit. It covers all RTA transport modes at standard fares and is the right choice for the vast majority of tourists.\n\n### Gold Nol Card\n\nThe Gold Nol Card also costs AED 25 with AED 19 in travel credit, but it grants access to the Gold Class carriages on the Dubai Metro and the Dubai Tram. Gold Class fares are charged at double the standard rate, so this card suits travellers who want a premium, less crowded journey and are happy to pay a little more for it.\n\n### Red Nol Ticket\n\nThe Red Nol Ticket is a single-use card costing AED 2 per purchase, with no pre-loaded credit. You add fare value for individual trips and it can hold up to ten single-journey rides, valid for 90 days. It is a practical option if you are only making a handful of Metro trips and do not want a stored-value card.\n\n### Blue Nol Card\n\nThe Blue Nol Card is linked to an Emirates ID and is issued to residents who qualify for concessionary fares — including students, senior citizens, and People of Determination. It is not generally applicable to tourists, but it is useful to know it exists if you fall into one of those categories.\n\nOne practical tip: the Nol Card offers a free transfer if you board a connecting bus within 30 minutes of tapping out at a Metro station. Plan your journeys with that window in mind to keep costs down.\n\n## The Dubai Metro\n\nThe Dubai Metro is the backbone of the city's public transport system. It is fully automated, entirely air-conditioned, and runs on two interlinking lines covering 50 stations across the city. Trains operate every 2 to 4 minutes during peak hours and every 5 to 7 minutes during off-peak times, making waits short and journeys predictable.\n\nPlanning your trip to Dubai starts with knowing which line takes you where. Whether you are heading to the beach, the _souk_, or a skyscraper observation deck, there is likely a Metro station within walking distance of your destination.\n\n## Discover Flights With Traveloka\n\nTue, 24 Nov 2026\n\n![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/image/imageResource/2023/10/23/1698049654067-f06979c2b4fb31ea86fc9a8970c0da7b.png?tr=q-75,w-50)\n\nSaudia\n\nRiyadh (RUH) to Dubai (DXB)\n\nStart from USD 54.78\n\nBook Now\n\nWed, 25 Nov 2026\n\n![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/image/imageResource/2024/09/25/1727240468931-5d603f94d079b16372f8a80844f7e113.png?tr=q-75,w-50)\n\nCebu Pacific\n\nManila (MNL) to Dubai (DXB)\n\nStart from USD 191.33\n\nBook Now\n\nSun, 8 Nov 2026\n\n![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/image/imageResource/2025/12/03/1764758836364-3f7a66d4c168e9177372d70179c19d06.png?tr=q-75,w-50)\n\nAir India\n\nSingapore (SIN) to Dubai (DXB)\n\nStart from USD 205.44\n\nBook Now\n\n### Red Line\n\nThe Red Line is the longer of the two lines, running from Centrepoint Station in Rashidiya in the east all the way south to Expo City Dubai. It passes through the most visited areas of the city, including Union interchange station, BurJuman, the World Trade Centre, Financial Centre, Mall of the Emirates, Dubai Marina, and Jumeirah Lake Towers. If you are staying along Sheikh Zayed Road or in the Marina area, the Red Line will be your most-used route.\n\n### Green Line\n\nThe Green Line runs from Etisalat Station in Al Qusais to Creek Station in Al Jaddaf, threading through the older, more historically rich parts of the city. It serves Al Fahidi (the historic district), Gold Souk Station, Baniyas Square, and Deira. Visitors interested in Dubai's heritage, traditional markets, and the _Creek_ waterfront will find the Green Line essential. The two lines interchange at Union and BurJuman stations.\n\n### Carriages and Cabins\n\nEach Metro train has designated carriages for different passengers. Silver Class carriages are open to all passengers. Gold Class carriages at the front of the train are reserved for Gold Nol Card holders. There is also a dedicated Women and Children's cabin on every train, which is clearly marked and exclusively for women and children at all times — male passengers are not permitted in this carriage regardless of crowding.\n\n### Metro Fares\n\nMetro fares with a Silver Nol Card are calculated by zone: AED 3 for one zone, AED 5 for two zones, and AED 7.50 for three or more zones. Gold Class travel is charged at double these rates. The daily spending cap on a Nol Card is AED 20, which means your transport costs are automatically capped no matter how many trips you make in a single day.\n\n## Getting to and from Dubai Airport by Metro\n\nDubai International Airport (DXB) is directly connected to the Metro via the Red Line, making it one of the most airport-accessible cities in the world. Two stations serve the airport: Airport Terminal 1 Station and Airport Terminal 3 Station, both on the Red Line. Terminal 1 serves flydubai and other airlines, while Terminal 3 is the main hub for Emirates and several other carriers.\n\nFrom Airport Terminal 3, you can ride the Red Line directly to Union Station (the main interchange with the Green Line), to the Dubai Mall area, to Mall of the Emirates, and all the way to Dubai Marina — all without changing trains. Nol Card vending machines are available inside both airport terminal stations, so you can purchase your card immediately after clearing arrivals.\n\nSearching for the right hotel before you fly makes the rest of the trip easier. Here are some great places to stay across Dubai, from budget-friendly options to luxury resorts.\n\n![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/image/imageResource/2020/10/27/1603791006056-0fc93a0066574a5de96895781f042eb5.png?tr=q-75,w-16,h-16,dpr-1.5)\n\nZa'abeel 128.41\n\n## The Dubai Tram\n\nThe Dubai Tram runs along Al Sufouh Road, covering 10.6 kilometres across 11 stations. It connects the Dubai Marina and Jumeirah Beach Residence ( _JBR_) areas with Al Sufouh, serving some of the city's most popular beach and residential districts. The tram integrates directly with the Metro Red Line at DMCC (Jumeirah Lake Towers) and Sobha Realty stations, making transfers straightforward.\n\nThe tram also links to the Palm Monorail at the Palm Jumeirah station, providing onward access to Atlantis The Palm and other Palm Jumeirah destinations. Nol Cards are accepted on the tram, and the same fare zones apply. Like the Metro, the tram has Gold Class sections for Gold Nol Card holders.\n\n## RTA Buses\n\nThe RTA bus network is the most extensive part of Dubai's public transport system, with over 1,500 buses operating across more than 100 routes. The network reaches 82% of the city's urban area, making it the only public transport option for neighbourhoods not served by the Metro or tram. Buses operate from around 4:00 AM to 1:00 AM, with some routes running 24 hours.\n\nBus fares use the same Nol Card and the same zone-based pricing as the Metro: AED 3 for one zone, AED 5 for two zones, and AED 7.50 for three or more zones. The 30-minute free transfer window from the Metro applies to bus journeys too, so connecting trips from the Metro to a bus cost nothing if timed correctly.\n\nOne distinctive feature of Dubai's bus system is its air-conditioned bus shelters. Given summer temperatures that regularly exceed 40°C, the RTA has installed air-conditioned waiting areas at hundreds of stops across the city, complete with real-time digital arrival boards. These shelters are fully accessible and make waiting for a bus a genuinely comfortable experience even in the hottest months.\n\n## Water Transport: Dubai Creek and the Marina\n\nDubai's water transport options range from the iconic traditional _abra_ to modern air-conditioned water buses, and they cover two distinct areas of the city: the historic Creek in Deira and Bur Dubai, and the modern marina districts along the coast.\n\n### Traditional Abra\n\nThe _abra_ is a traditional wooden motorboat that has been crossing Dubai Creek for generations. These vessels ferry passengers between the Deira and Bur Dubai sides of the Creek, and the fare is just AED 1 per person, paid in cash to the _abbara_ (boatman) on board. For visitors, it is one of the most atmospheric and affordable ways to experience old Dubai, gliding past the _dhow_ wharves with views of the historic waterfront.\n\n### Water Bus\n\nThe RTA water bus service operates on Dubai Creek and at several marina stations, offering covered, air-conditioned journeys along the water. Nol Cards are accepted on water buses, and fares start from AED 3 per journey. The water bus is a practical option for reaching Creek-side attractions and offers a more scenic alternative to road transport in the Deira area.\n\n### Water Taxi\n\nThe RTA water taxi service offers faster, point-to-point journeys between selected marine stations across the city, including stations at the Dubai Marina and along the Creek. Fares are higher than the water bus and vary by route, but the service is air-conditioned and offers a comfortable, uncrowded way to travel along the waterfront. Bookings can be made through the RTA app or at marine stations.\n\n## The Palm Monorail\n\nThe Palm Monorail runs along the trunk of Palm Jumeirah, connecting the Gateway station (which links to the Dubai Tram at Palm Jumeirah station) to Atlantis Aquaventure station at the far end of the Palm. The monorail is not operated by the RTA, and Nol Cards are not accepted — tickets are purchased separately at the station. It is primarily a tourist connection, providing a convenient and elevated way to travel the length of the Palm without a car or taxi.\n\n## Free Shuttle Buses at Major Malls\n\nSeveral of Dubai's largest malls and hotels run their own complimentary shuttle services connecting their premises to nearby Metro stations. These are worth checking before your visit, as they can save both time and taxi fare, particularly if you are staying in a hotel that is not directly on a Metro line. Shuttle schedules are typically available at hotel concierge desks and on mall websites.\n\n## Top Tips for Using Public Transport in Dubai\n\nA few practical points make the whole system easier to navigate from day one.\n\n### Buy Your Nol Card at the Airport\n\nNol Card vending machines are located inside both DXB Metro stations, accessible immediately after you clear arrivals and before you exit the terminal. Picking up a Silver Nol Card here means you can board the Metro straight away without hunting for a machine later.\n\n### Check the Women and Children's Cabin\n\nThe Women and Children's cabin on the Metro is located at a specific end of the train, clearly marked by signage on the platform and on the train doors. Female travellers are welcome to use either Silver Class or the Women and Children's cabin — the dedicated cabin tends to be quieter and less crowded during peak hours.\n\n### Respect Quiet and Eating Rules\n\nEating, drinking, and smoking are prohibited on the Metro and in all Metro stations. These rules are enforced and fines apply. Keep snacks in your bag until you exit the station.\n\n### Use the S'hail App for Journey Planning\n\nThe RTA's official journey planning app consolidates real-time timetables, route maps, and Nol Card top-up options for the Metro, bus, tram, and water transport into a single interface. It is the most reliable tool for planning multi-mode journeys across the city.\n\n### Top Up Your Nol Card Regularly\n\nYou can top up your Nol Card at any Metro station vending machine, through the Nol Pay app, or at selected convenience stores. If you top up online, tap your card at a station gate or machine to activate the credit before you travel — online top-ups require a physical activation step.\n\nBeyond getting around, Dubai has an extraordinary range of experiences to book before you go. From the world's largest observation wheel to indoor skiing and aquarium tours, here are some of the top activities available through Traveloka.\n\n![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/image/imageResource/2020/10/27/1603791006056-0fc93a0066574a5de96895781f042eb5.png?tr=q-75,w-16,h-16,dpr-1.5)\n\nDubai\n\n![](https://img.cnt.traveloka.com/tvlk/xpe-asset/GZtqLuOqb-33qvDmG1FcMeN1eFEH7PVvlsdFr2AUBT39jzSxIGG5kZNhhHY-p7nw/9477245604102/Skydive-Dubai-Tandem-Skydiving-Experience-724b07f9-ef14-4f68-bc2a-36bd8f8ad9de.jpeg?tr=q-70,h-400,w-500)\n\nSkydive Traveloka\n\n[Traveloka](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en?funnel_source=content_article&funnel_id=WP_1010730) is Southeast Asia's leading travel platform, trusted by over 100 million users to plan and book trips across Asia and beyond. Whether you are flying into Dubai, searching for the right hotel in the Marina or Downtown, or looking for [activities](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/activities?funnel_source=content_article&funnel_id=WP_1010730) to fill your itinerary, everything is in one place.\n\nBook [flights](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/flight?funnel_source=content_article&funnel_id=WP_1010730) and compare hundreds of options to find the best fare for your Dubai trip. Once you land, browse a wide range of [hotels](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/hotel?funnel_source=content_article&funnel_id=WP_1010730) — from design-forward city properties on Sheikh Zayed Road to beachfront resorts on Palm Jumeirah — and filter by price, location, and rating to find the right fit. If you are planning to explore beyond the Metro network, [car rental](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/car-rental?funnel_source=content_article&funnel_id=WP_1010730) options are available for day trips and weekend escapes to nearby destinations. For getting to and from the airport with ease, [airport transfer](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/airport-transfer?funnel_source=content_article&funnel_id=WP_1010730) options are bookable in advance so you arrive without the stress of arranging transport on arrival.\n\nKeep an eye on [Traveloka promotions](https://www.traveloka.com/en-en/promotion?funnel_source=content_article&funnel_id=WP_1010730) for exclusive deals on flights, hotels, and activities to Dubai. The app also covers travel insurance and eSIM for international data — useful additions for a seamless trip. [![partners](https://www.rakta.gov.ae/wp-content/themes/rakta/assets/images/Aurak%20logo.png)](https://aurak.ac.ae/)\n\n## Safe commuting by public transportation\n\n## International Bus\n\nRAKTA provides safe international public transportation services that connect the Emirate of Ras Al Khaimah with the Governorate of Musandam in the Sultanate of Oman to promote public transportation and enhance shared mobility as a sustainable land transport solution.\n\n##### Sayr for Booking\n\n[![](https://www.rakta.gov.ae/wp-content/themes/rakta/assets/images/playstore.png)](http://bit.ly/sayr) [![](https://www.rakta.gov.ae/wp-content/themes/rakta/assets/images/appstore.png)](http://bit.ly/sayr)\n\n## From Rak\n\n- ##### RAK To Musandam\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nDays\n\n\n\n###### Friday, fast, convenient, safe and reliable experience\n\n## RAK Ride\n\nRAKTA provides the Express Bus Service Solution “RAK Ride,” which provides direct, non-stop transportation services that operate on main operational lines with multiple paths linking several areas in the emirate, especially remote areas to the city center, and provides transportation and access from the first point to the last mile fast and efficient to enable users to reach their destinations quickly, comfortably and safely.\n\n- [Timings](https://www.rakta.gov.ae/public-transport/#first0)\n- [Stopping points](https://www.rakta.gov.ae/public-transport/#second0)\n- [Tariff](https://www.rakta.gov.ae/public-transport/#third0)\n- [Payment method](https://www.rakta.gov.ae/public-transport/#fourth0)\n\n| transportation fees through the internal public buses\n\n![](https://www.rakta.gov.ae/wp-content/themes/rakta/assets/images/card3.png)\n\n##### Blue Card\n\nFor all categories and users\n\n###### Card purchase value 30 AED including the 20 dirhams for the e-wallet value for the first time.\n\n![](https://www.rakta.gov.ae/wp-content/themes/rakta/assets/images/card1.png)\n\n##### Silver Card\n\nUniversity and school students\nSenior citizens and residents (above 60 years old)\n\n###### Card purchase value 20 AED including 50% discount on the trip fees.\n\n![](https://www.rakta.gov.ae/wp-content/themes/rakta/assets/images/card2.png)\n\n##### Gold Card\n\nPeople of determination category\n\n###### Lifetime Free Card\n\n##### How to complete the transaction\n\n- 1 – The customer goes to the ticket counter at the main bus station\n- 2 – Requesting the card type (blue-gold-silver) from the station employee\n- 3 – Pay the fees\n- 4 – Receive the card\n\n##### Card sales points\n\n###### Blue card\n\n- 1 – Inside the bus\n- 2 – Ticket counter at the main bus station\n\n###### Gold and Silver card\n\n- 1 – Ticket counter at the main bus station\n\n##### Toll Free:\n\n800 1700\n\n##### Email:\n\ninfo@rakta.gov.ae\n\n##### Working Hours:\n\nMon - Thu 7:30am - 3:30pm\n\nFriday 7:30am - 12:00pm\n\n##### Location:\n\n[View Location in         "url": "https://uk.trip.com/guide/transport/dubai-airport-to-city.html",
        "title": "How to Get from Dubai Airport to City Centre - Trip.com",
        "description": "# How to Get from Dubai Airport to City Center\n## 🚍 Transport Options from Dubai Airport to the City Centre\n|  |  |  |  |  |\n|-|-|-|-|-|\n| **Transport (DXB → city centre)** | **Duration** | **Approx. cost** | **Comfort** | **Perfect for** |\n| **Private transfer** | 15 – 40 mins | from ~90 – 150 AED (≈ £19 – £32) | ⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ | Families, groups, late-night arrivals, first-time visitors |\n| **RTA Taxi** | 15 – 40 mins | 50 – 70 AED to Downtown (≈ £10 – £15) | ⭐⭐⭐⭐ | Direct journey, no booking required |\n| **Private hire vehicles (Careem / Uber)** | 15 – 40 mins | close to the taxi, price displayed upfront | ⭐⭐⭐⭐ | Transparent pricing via the app |\n| **Metro (Red Line)** | ~25 mins | 5 – 7.50 AED (≈ £1.00 – £1.60) | ⭐⭐⭐ | Small budgets, light luggage, T1/T3 |\n| **RTA Bus** | 40 – 60 mins | 3 – 8 AED (≈ £0.60 – £1.70) | ⭐⭐ | Very small budgets |\n| **Car hire** | variable | from ~120 AED/day (≈ £25) | ⭐⭐⭐ | Long stays, excursions |\n\n## 🚗 Dubai Airport to the City Centre by Airport Transfer\n### 💸 How much does it cost?\nExpect to pay generally between **90 and 150 AED** (around £19 to £32) depending on the vehicle, from a saloon to a family MPV. The price is known in advance and is fixed, regardless of traffic.\n\n**📱 The benefits of private transport from Dubai Airport**\n\n## 🚕 Dubai Airport to the City Centre by Taxi\n**Taxis** remain the most direct option if you don't want to book anything in advance. At DXB, the official cream-coloured **RTA taxis** are available 24/7 outside every terminal.\n\nFollow the \" **TAXI**\" signs and join the organised queue: no haggling, the meter is final.\n\n## 🚙 Dubai Airport to the City Centre by Ride-Hailing\nThe _Careem_ (the local player, acquired by Uber) and **Uber** apps work perfectly in Dubai. Their main benefit? The price is displayed before you book, which removes any uncertainty.\n\nCareem's \" **Hala Taxi**\" option even lets you book a standard RTA taxi via the app, at the regulated fare.\n\n## 🚇 Dubai Airport to the City Centre by Metro\nThe Dubai Metro is the most economical option and, during peak hours, possibly the fastest. The Red Line serves the city centre directly, with no changes required.\n\n**📍 Essential things to know**\n\n- Metro stations are only located at **Terminals 1 and 3**. Terminal 2 doesn't have a station, so you'll need to take a taxi or a bus from T2.\n- For **Downtown**, hop on the **Red Line** and get off at the Burj Khalifa / Dubai Mall station (about twenty minutes, no changes required).\n- The metro is cashless, so make sure to pick up a **Nol card**. The Silver card costs 25 AED (which includes 19 AED of usable credit), and a limited-use Red ticket costs 2 AED plus the price of your journey.\n- **Fares are calculated by zone**: a trip from the airport to Downtown costs between 5 and 7.50 AED (£1.00 to £1.60), with a daily cap of 14 AED on the Silver card.\n- Trains run approximately **every 4 minutes** during peak hours, and every 7 minutes off-peak.\n\n## 🚌 Dubai Airport to the City Centre by Express Bus\nFor those on a tight budget, **RTA buses** serve Terminals 1 and 3. Using the same Nol card as the metro, the journey costs just 3 to 8 AED (around £0.60 to £1.70).\n\nIt’s unbeatable on price, but significantly **slower**: you have to contend with stops, potential transfers, and sometimes heavy traffic, meaning 40 to 60 minutes to the city centre. If you have bulky luggage, it’s not ideal.\n\nThis option is mainly for travellers with no time constraints who want to spend as little as possible.\n\n## 🧳 Best Way to Get from Dubai Airport to the City Centre\n- **Families with children**: a private transfer remains the best option: door-to-door service, child seats available, space for luggage, and no waiting in taxi queues.\n- **Tight budget, travelling light**: the metro for a daytime journey to a central station; the bus if every dirham counts and the schedule matters little.\n- **Arriving in the middle of the night?** A private transfer, without hesitation. The metro is closed, and having a driver waiting for you, with a confirmed booking in hand, is far better than trying to find a solution at 2 am.\n- **Business traveller or looking for comfort**: a private transfer or ride-hailing service is the way to go for a direct journey at a controlled price, with no need to handle cash.\n- **A trip focused on exploration**: hire a car to freely travel between Abu Dhabi, Hatta, or the desert.\n\n## ❓ FAQ – Dubai Airport to City Centre\nA pre-booked private transfer: pick-up at your terminal, fixed price, flight tracking, and door-to-door drop-off at your hotel – available 24/7. No queues, no surprises.\n\nExpect around 50–70 AED (£10–15) to Downtown, more to Dubai Marina. Airport pick-up starts at 25 AED (£5.50).",

        "markdown": "[Trip.com](https://uk.trip.com/?locale=en-GB&curr=GBP)\n\nApp\n\nGBP\n\nCustomer support\n\n[Find bookings](https://uk.trip.com/passport/ordersearch/?locale=en-GB&curr=GBP)\n\nSign in/register\n\n# How to Get from Dubai Airport to City Center\n\n![Author's Profile Picture](https://ak-d.tripcdn.com/images/0wj6h120009xetovo4893_C_60_60.png_.webp)\n\nBy Tina\n\nJuly 3, 2026\n\nAirport Transportation\n\n![How to Get from Dubai Airport to City Center](https://dimg04.tripcdn.com/images/0M74s12000t0srdcy0D39_Q60.jpg?proc=format/f_webp)\n\n**Fifteen kilometres**! That's all that separates **Dubai International Airport (DXB)**, in **Al Garhoud**, from the foot of the Burj Khalifa. In other words, **Downtown**, the **Marina** or the old town are practically on your doorstep as soon as you step off the plane. The real question, therefore, isn't the distance, but how to get there: should you choose the metro, a taxi, a ride-hailing service, a private transfer or the bus?\n\nIt all depends on your budget, the time you land, and how many suitcases you're travelling with. We'll break it down, option by option, in this detailed guide.\n\n## 🚍 Transport Options from Dubai Airport to the City Centre\n\nIt’s no surprise that **DXB** is one of the busiest airports on the planet, and it has an advantage that’s rare among other airports: it’s right in the middle of the city!\n\nWhile other capitals have airports located an hour's drive away, in Dubai, you can get to the city centre in twenty minutes outside of peak hours. Before we go into the details, here is a table comparing the main transport options for getting to the city centre:\n\n|     |     |     |     |     |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| **Transport (DXB → city centre)** | **Duration** | **Approx. cost** | **Comfort** | **Perfect for** |\n| **Private transfer** | 15 – 40 mins | from ~90 – 150 AED (≈ £19 – £32) | ⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ | Families, groups, late-night arrivals, first-time visitors |\n| **RTA Taxi** | 15 – 40 mins | 50 – 70 AED to Downtown (≈ £10 – £15) | ⭐⭐⭐⭐ | Direct journey, no booking required |\n| **Private hire vehicles (Careem / Uber)** | 15 – 40 mins | close to the taxi, price displayed upfront | ⭐⭐⭐⭐ | Transparent pricing via the app |\n| **Metro (Red Line)** | ~25 mins | 5 – 7.50 AED (≈ £1.00 – £1.60) | ⭐⭐⭐ | Small budgets, light luggage, T1/T3 |\n| **RTA Bus** | 40 – 60 mins | 3 – 8 AED (≈ £0.60 – £1.70) | ⭐⭐ | Very small budgets |\n| **Car hire** | variable | from ~120 AED/day (≈ £25) | ⭐⭐⭐ | Long stays, excursions |\n\nDiscover cheap flights to Dubai\n\nFrom London\n\nFrom Manchester\n\nFrom Birmingham\n\nSee all flights\n\n[![easyJet](https://pic.tripcdn.com/airline_logo/3x/u2.png)\\\\\n\\\\\nLondon\\\\\n\\\\\nLGW\\|07:20\\\\\n\\\\\n17h 5m\\\\\n\\\\\n2 stops\\\\\n\\\\\nDubai\\\\\n\\\\\nDXB\\|04:25+1\\\\\n\\\\\nThu, 5 Nov -
