Wed, 18 Nov\\\\\n\\\\\nOne way\\\\\n\\\\\n21% off\\\\\n\\\\\nFrom 200.00£159.00\\\\\n\\\\\nBook now](https://uk.trip.com/flights/Birmingham-to-Dubai/tickets-BHX-DXB?sorttype=&ddate=2026-11-16&adate=2026-11-18&currency=GBP&flighttype=s)\n\n## 🚗 Dubai Airport to the City Centre by Airport Transfer\n\nIf you only choose one option for a completely seamless arrival, this is the one. With a **private transfer**, a driver will be waiting for you in the arrivals hall with a sign bearing your name, will take care of your luggage, and drive you directly to your hotel door.\n\nNo queues, no apps to set up as soon as you step off the plane, no fares to confirm in a foreign language. In short, the ideal option!\n\nThis is particularly appreciated in Dubai, where a large number of long-haul flights land late at night or in the early hours, at times when the metro is no longer running.\n\nFor example, the [**Trip.com airport transfer**](https://uk.trip.com/ask/questions/book-airport-transfers.html) can be booked entirely online before you depart: the price is fixed at the time of booking, and the driver tracks your flight status to adjust the pick-up time in case of delays.\n\n### 🕒 Duration and availability\n\n- **Journey time**: 15 to 25 minutes to Downtown off-peak, up to 40 minutes during rush hour.\n- **Availability**: 24/7, every day. An ideal option for late-night arrivals.\n\n### 💸 How much does it cost?\n\nExpect to pay generally between **90 and 150 AED** (around £19 to £32) depending on the vehicle, from a saloon to a family MPV. The price is known in advance and is fixed, regardless of traffic.\n\n**📱 The benefits of private transport from Dubai Airport**\n\n- A fixed price, displayed at the time of booking, with no meter and no last-minute surcharges.\n- A door-to-door service, from the terminal right to your hotel reception.\n- Integrated flight tracking, even if your landing is delayed.\n- Air-conditioned, spacious vehicles with plenty of room for luggage—a total relief when you're arriving in 40°C heat!\n- Child seats are available on request, and people carriers for groups.\n\n**✅ Which travellers is this the right choice for?**\n\nFor families laden with suitcases, for a first visit to Dubai, for late arrivals, and quite simply for anyone who prefers to relax the moment they step off the plane.\n\n✈️ Find [**cheap flights to Dubai**](https://uk.trip.com/flights/to-dubai/airfares-dxb/) and start your journey effortlessly\n\n🏨 Book your [**hotels in Dubai**](https://uk.trip.com/hotels/dubai-hotels-list-220/) to plan your itineraries perfectly\n\n⭐ Buy a [**Dubai eSIM**](https://uk.trip.com/things-to-do/detail/73727601/) to enjoy unlimited internet access\n\n❤️ Discover the best [**attractions and tours in Dubai**](https://uk.trip.com/travel-guide/destination/dubai-1062/) and enjoy [**5% OFF**](https://uk.trip.com/guide/transport/dubai-airport-to-city.html)\n\n[Book your airport transfer in Dubai](https://uk.trip.com/airport-transfers/)\n\n## 🚕 Dubai Airport to the City Centre by Taxi\n\n**Taxis** remain the most direct option if you don't want to book anything in advance. At DXB, the official cream-coloured **RTA taxis** are available 24/7 outside every terminal.\n\nFollow the \" **TAXI**\" signs and join the organised queue: no haggling, the meter is final.\n\n### 🚖 Prices and things to know\n\nAirport pick-ups start at 25 AED (around £5.50), a slightly higher rate than hailing one on the street. For Downtown, expect to pay 50 to 70 AED (£10 to £15); for the further-away Marina, more like 90 to 100 AED (£19 to £21). Most taxis accept card payments, and it's wise to ask for a receipt.\n\n### 🧳 Who is this for?\n\nFor those who want to set off immediately, without an app or booking, and for daytime journeys to the centre where the fare remains reasonable.\n\n**⚠️ Keep in mind**\n\n**Prices climb quickly** during rush hour due to traffic jams, and a large group with lots of luggage will sometimes have to wait for a larger vehicle (often charged at a higher rate). For a price locked in advance, a private transfer remains more predictable!\n\nGet Your eSIM for UAE Now\n\nSee all player, acquired by Uber) and **Uber** apps work perfectly in Dubai. Their main benefit? The price is displayed before you book, which removes any uncertainty.\n\nCareem's \" **Hala Taxi**\" option even lets you book a standard RTA taxi via the app, at the regulated fare.\n\n### 📍 How does it work at the airport?\n\nPick-ups take place from **dedicated chauffeur vehicle zones**, which are signposted in every terminal. The app will show you exactly where to meet your driver.\n\nPrices are similar to those of a taxi, sometimes slightly higher during periods of high demand (dynamic pricing).\n\n### 🧳 Who is this for?\n\nFor travellers already accustomed to these apps who appreciate knowing the price and tracking their journey in real time.\n\n**⚠️ Keep in mind**\n\nDuring peak demand, prices can climb higher than a standard taxi. Remember to have a **connection** ready as soon as you land (an eSIM bought before you leave sorts this out) so you can book without hunting for Wi-Fi.\n\n🎁 Exclusive Offers For You:\n\nHotels: Save City Centre by Metro\n\nThe Dubai Metro is the most economical option and, during peak hours, possibly the fastest. The Red Line serves the city centre directly, with no changes required.\n\n**📍 Essential things to know**\n\n- Metro stations are only located at **Terminals 1 and 3**. Terminal 2 doesn't have a station, so you'll need to take a taxi or a bus from T2.\n- For **Downtown**, hop on the **Red Line** and get off at the Burj Khalifa / Dubai Mall station (about twenty minutes, no changes required).\n- The metro is cashless, so make sure to pick up a **Nol card**. The Silver card costs 25 AED (which includes 19 AED of usable credit), and a limited-use Red ticket costs 2 AED plus the price of your journey.\n- **Fares are calculated by zone**: a trip from the airport to Downtown costs between 5 and 7.50 AED (£1.00 to £1.60), with a daily cap of 14 AED on the Silver card.\n- Trains run approximately **every 4 minutes** during peak hours, and every 7 minutes off-peak.\n\n### 🕒 Opening hours\n\nThe Red Line runs from 5 am to midnight Saturday to Wednesday, until 1 am on Thursday, and starts later on Friday (around 10 am, until 1 am). As you’ve probably gathered, for a late-night arrival, the metro isn't an option, and you'll need to look into a taxi or private transfer.\n\n### 🧳 Who is this for?\n\nFor travellers on a **tight budget**, travelling light, and whose hotel is located near a station.\n\n_**Please note**_ _: only two pieces of luggage per person are allowed on board, which makes this option a bit tricky if you're travelling with large suitcases._\n\n[Get your Dubai eSIM at the best price](https://uk.trip.com/things-to-do/detail/73727601/)\n\n[Best hotels in Dubai](https://uk.trip.com/hotels/dubai-hotels-list-220/)\n\n## 🚌 Dubai Airport to the City Centre by Express Bus\n\nFor those on a tight budget, **RTA buses** serve Terminals 1 and 3. Using the same Nol card as the metro, the journey costs just 3 to 8 AED (around £0.60 to £1.70).\n\nIt’s unbeatable on price, but significantly **slower**: you have to contend with stops, potential transfers, and sometimes heavy traffic, meaning 40 to 60 minutes to the city centre. If you have bulky luggage, it’s not ideal.\n\nThis option is mainly for travellers with no time constraints who want to spend as little as possible.\n\n## 🚘 Car Rental from Dubai Airport to the City Centre\n\nSeveral **international and local agencies** have desks at DXB. Hiring a car really makes sense for a stay of several days or for travelling beyond the city: to Abu Dhabi, Hatta, or the desert roads.\n\n### 💸 Prices and things to watch out for\n\nFrom around **120 AED per day** (£25) for a small car, excluding extras.\n\nKeep in mind that they **drive on the right** in the Emirates, but also that there is paid parking in the city centre and a massive number of speed cameras: an up-to-date GPS and strict adherence to speed limits are highly recommended.\n\nFor a simple transfer on your arrival day, right after a long flight, renting a car isn't the best idea; save it for the rest of your trip instead.\n\nCar Rentals\n\n8% off Car Rental\n\nNew users only.\n\nTRIPCAR8\n\nCopy\n\n[Hire a car in Dubai](https://uk.trip.com/carhire/offres-location-de-voiture-dubai/)\n\n## 🧳 Best Way to Get from Dubai Airport to the City Centre\n\nThe right choice depends above all on your **specific needs and the reasons for your trip**. Here is a quick summary:\n\n- **Families with children**: a private transfer remains the best option: door-to-door service, child seats available, space for luggage, and no waiting in taxi queues.\n- **Tight budget, travelling light**: the metro for a daytime journey to a central station; the bus if every dirham counts and the schedule matters little.\n- **Arriving in the middle of the night?** A private transfer, without hesitation. The metro is closed, and having a driver waiting for you, with a confirmed booking in hand, is far better than trying to find a solution at 2 am.\n- **Business traveller or looking for comfort**: a private transfer or ride-hailing service is the way to go for a direct journey at a controlled price, with no need to handle cash.\n- **A trip focused on exploration**: hire a car to freely travel between Abu Dhabi, Hatta, or the desert.\n\n**Must-see sights and tickets to book**\n\n🏙️ [**Burj Khalifa – \"At the Top\" observation decks (124th & 125th floors)**](https://uk.trip.com/travel-guide/attraction/dubai/burj-khalifa-87338/) ⭐ The view of Dubai from the world's tallest skyscraper\n\n🖼️ [**Dubai Frame – the giant frame between past and future**](https://uk.trip.com/travel-guide/attraction/dubai/dubai-frame-46832162/) 📸 A panorama of old and new Dubai\n\n**Things to do and experiences in Dubai**\n\n🏜️ [**Desert safari**](https://uk.trip.com/things-to-do/detail/103601524?language=FR&locale=fr_fr&districtId=1062&districtType=1&ext-list=city_category_card_seo&ext-seoslug=dubai): dune bashing, camels and barbecue dinner 🔥 The quintessential Emirati experience\n\n⛵ All [**tours and tickets in Dubai**](https://uk.trip.com/things-to-do/experiences/dubai/) — dhow cruise, aquarium, Abu Dhabi and more\n\n[Attractions & Tours in Dubai](https://uk.trip.com/travel-guide/destination/dubai-1062/)\n\n## ℹ️ About Dubai International Airport (DXB)\n\n![](https://dimg04.tripcdn.com/images/1n90u424x99ko9g2wDCBE_Q60.jpg?proc=format/f_webp)\n\nSome practical, up-to-date information to help you prepare for your arrival:\n\n- **Three terminals**: **T1** hosts the majority of international airlines, **T3** is the giant Emirates hub (one of the largest passenger terminals in the world), and T2, located separately to the north, serves flydubai among others. Terminals 1 and 3 are connected and served by the metro; **T2** is not.\n- **A second airport**: Dubai also has **Al Maktoum Airport (DWC)**, in the south of the emirate, which is used by some airlines. If your ticket shows DWC, the journey to the centre is much longer: check your airport code before you leave. This guide concerns **DXB**, the airport in the city.\n- **Situation as of** **June 2026**: DXB is fully operational and the UAE airspace is open. As the regional situation remains fluid, simply confirm your flight with your airline before heading to the airport.\n- **Tourist tax**: hotels apply a \" **Tourism Dirham**\" which is paid upon departure (ranging from a few dirhams to around twenty per night, depending on the category). This isn't an airport tax, but it's worth factoring into your budget.\n\n[Cheap flights to Dubai](https://uk.trip.com/flights/to-dubai/airfares-dxb/)\n\n[Trip.com travel deals](https://uk.trip.com/hot/trip.com-promo-code/)\n\nWhatever your travel style, there's a perfect option for getting to the centre of Dubai. And for a truly stress-free arrival, a private transfer remains the reliable choice.\n\n## ❓ FAQ – Dubai Airport to City Centre\n\n- ### How far is Dubai city centre from the airport?\n\n\n\n\n\n\nDubai International Airport (DXB) is about 15 km from Downtown Dubai. The journey takes 15–25 minutes outside peak hours, and up to 40 minutes during rush hour (typically 7–9 am and 5–8 pm).\n\n\n- ### What's the most hassle-free way to get from Dubai Airport to the city?\n\n\n\n\n\n\nA pre-booked private transfer: pick-up at your terminal, fixed price, flight tracking, and door-to-door drop-off at your hotel – available 24/7. No queues, no surprises.\n\n\n- ### Does the Dubai Metro run from the airport to the city centre?\n\n\n\n\n\n\nYes. The Red Line serves Terminals 1 and 3 and connects directly to Downtown Dubai (Burj Khalifa / Dubai Mall station) with no changes. Note: Terminal 2 doesn't have a station, and the metro doesn't run overnight.\n\n\n- ### How much is a taxi from Dubai Airport to the city centre?\n\n\n\n\n\n\nExpect around 50–70 AED (£10–15) to Downtown, more to Dubai Marina. Airport pick-up starts at 25 AED (£5.50).\n\n\n- ### Can I pay for the Dubai Metro with cash?\n\n\n\n\n\n\nNo. The metro is cashless – you'll need a Nol Card (or a Red Ticket), available from vending machines and ticket counters at Terminals 1 and 3.\n\n\n\n## 🧩 Discover Dubai\n\n[Where to Stay in Dubai: Best Areas, Hotels & Attractions\\\\\n\\\\\nWhere to stay in Dubai?         "description": "# How to Travel from Dubai to Abu Dhabi via Car, Train, Bus, and Flight\n## Bus Journey from Dubai to Abu Dhabi\nPublic bus service offers the most economical way **to go to Abu Dhabi from Dubai**. The Roads and Transport Authority (RTA) operates comfortable, air-conditioned buses with excellent connectivity.\n\n### Dubai to Abu Dhabi bus ticket price\nThe **Dubai to Abu Dhabi bus ticket price** is AED 25 per journey. Payment requires a Nol card, the same smart card used throughout Dubai's public transportation system.\n\n**Nol card requirements:**\n\n- Minimum load: AED 25 for a one-way journey\n- Recommended load: AED 50 for a round trip\n- Available at: Bus stations, metro stations, selected retail outlets\n\n### Bus comfort and amenities\n- Air conditioning with individual controls\n- Reclining seats with ample legroom\n- USB charging ports and power outlets\n- Free Wi-Fi (limited coverage)\n- Under-bus luggage storage\n- Priority seating arrangements\n\nThe journey offers scenic views of the UAE's desert landscape and developing urban areas. Many travellers use this time to plan their Abu Dhabi itinerary or catch up on work.\n\n## Why There's No Direct Train or Flight from Dubai to Abu Dhabi\nMany visitors wonder about train or flight options for this route. Here's why these aren't available:\n\n### Railway infrastructure\nThe UAE lacks intercity passenger rail service.\n\n## Cost Comparison and Travel Times from Dubai to Abu Dhabi\n| Transport mode | Cost (one-way) | Travel time | Convenience level |\n|-|-|-|-|\n| Private Car/Taxi | AED 250-400 | 1hr 22min | High |\n| Bus (Direct) | AED 25 | 1hr 40min | Medium |\n| Metro + Bus | AED 28-33 | 2hr 30min | Low |\n| Car Rental | AED 100-150/day + fuel | 1hr 22min | High |\n\n## Key Takeaways\n- **Dubai to Abu Dhabi distance** by road is 140 kilometres, taking 1 hour 22 minutes by car under normal traffic conditions.\n- Bus travel costs just **AED 25** compared to **AED 250-400** for taxis, making it the most budget-friendly option.\n- Private car or taxi offers the best convenience and time efficiency for families and business travellers.\n- Avoid peak hours (7-9 AM and 5-7 PM) to prevent journey times extending to nearly 2 hours.\n- No advance booking required for buses — they run every 20 minutes between major stations.\n- **Abu Dhabi to Dubai metro fare** doesn't exist as direct rail service isn't available between the cities.\n\nFrequently Asked Questions\n\nHow long does it take to travel from Dubai to Abu Dhabi?\n\nThe fastest option is a private car or taxi at 1 hour 22 minutes. Bus travel takes approximately 1 hour and 40 minutes.\n\nWhat is the cheapest way to get from Dubai to Abu Dhabi?\n\nPublic bus service at AED 25 per journey offers the most economical option.\n\nIs there a train from Dubai to Abu Dhabi?\n\nNo direct train service exists. A metro-plus-bus combination is available but takes significantly longer.\n\nDo I need a visa to travel between Dubai and Abu Dhabi?\n\nNo, both cities are within the UAE. Simply arrive at the station with a loaded Nol card and board the next available bus.         "markdown": "![How to Travel From Dubai to Abu Dhabi Via Car, Train, Bus and Flight](https://cdn.alike.io/directus-images/6e84c380-dc36-492a-901b-5d8741b9afed.jpg?format=webp&quality=80)\n\nDubai\n\nUAE\n\n# How to Travel from Dubai to Abu Dhabi via Car, Train, Bus, and Flight\n\nAug 14, 2025\n\nBy Supriya Sahu\n\nPlanning your journey between the two iconic UAE cities? Whether you're visiting for business or exploring the capital's attractions, understanding your transportation options helps you make the right choice. The distance between Dubai and Abu Dhabi is approximately 140 kilometres by road, making it one of the most popular intercity routes in the Emirates.\n\n## Dubai to Abu Dhabi Distance and Travel Overview\n\nThe Dubai to Abu Dhabi distance varies depending on your chosen route and transportation method. Here's what you need to know:\n\n| Measurement type | Distance |\n| --- | --- |\n| Road Distance | 140 kilometers |\n| Aerial Distance | 116 kilometers (Flight distance is slightly longer, approx 130 km) |\n| Average Travel Time | 1 hour 22 minutes |\n\nThe journey between these cities is straightforward, with well-maintained highways and multiple transportation options. Unlike many international routes, flying isn't practical due to the short distance between Dubai and Abu Dhabi. Instead, ground transportation offers convenience, comfort, and cost-effectiveness.\n\n### Peak travel times and considerations\n\nTraffic patterns significantly impact your journey time. Rush hours (7-9 AM and 5-7 PM) can extend travel time to nearly two hours. Weekend mornings typically offer the smoothest rides, while Friday afternoons see heavier traffic as residents travel between cities.\n\nFor those planning to experience both cities' attractions, consider staying overnight rather than taking day trips. This approach maximises your time at destinations like the [Louvre Abu Dhabi](https://alike.io/products/louvre-museum-abu-dhabi) or [Ferrari World Abu Dhabi](https://alike.io/products/ferrari-world-abu-dhabi).\n\nVisiting Dubai and Abu Dhabi? [Let Eia plan both cities!](https://alike.io/?utm_source=blog&utm_medium=inline-cta&utm_campaign=eia-dubai-to-abu-dhabi)\n\n## Travelling by Car from Dubai to Abu Dhabi\n\nDriving remains the most flexible option for **how to travel from Dubai to Abu Dhabi**. Two primary routes connect the cities:\n\n### Route 1: Via Exit 11 (Sheikh Mohammed bin Zayed Road)\n\n- **Distance:** 111 kilometres\n\n- **Travel time:** 1 hour 2 minutes\n\n- **Key landmarks:** Deerfields Mall, Dubai Marina, Palm Jumeirah, Mall of the Emirates\n\n\n### Route 2: Via Sheikh Zayed Road (E11) and E311\n\n- **Distance:** 120 kilometres\n\n- **Travel time:** 1 hour 13 minutes\n\n- **Key landmarks:** Emirates Park Zoo, Ibn Battuta Mall, Jumeirah Lakes Towers\n\n\n### Car rental and taxi options\n\n**Taxi fares:** AED 250-400 for the complete journey\n\n**Car rental companies:** Budget Rent a Car, One Click Drive Dubai, Kayak\n\n**Fuel costs:** Approximately AED 40-50 for a round trip\n\nIf you're planning to explore multiple attractions in Abu Dhabi, consider combining your transportation with [the Official Abu Dhabi Pass](https://alike.io/products/abu-dhabi-culture-and-heritage-pass) for seamless access to major sites.\n\n### Driving tips for first-time visitors\n\nUAE driving standards are excellent, with clear signage in both Arabic and English. Speed limits are strictly enforced through automated cameras. Most rental cars come equipped with Salik (toll) tags for seamless highway travel.\n\n## Bus Journey from Dubai to Abu Dhabi\n\nPublic bus service offers the most economical way **to go to Abu Dhabi from Dubai**. The Roads and Transport Authority (RTA) operates comfortable, air-conditioned buses with excellent connectivity.\n\n### Bus routes and schedules\n\n| Route | Starting point | Ending point | Frequency | Travel time |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| E100 | Al Ghubaiba Bus Station | Abu Dhabi Central Bus Station | Every 20 minutes | 1 hour 40 minutes |\n| E101 | Ibn Battuta Metro Station | Abu Dhabi Central Bus Station | Every 20 minutes | 1 hour 40 minutes |\n\n### Dubai to Abu Dhabi bus ticket price\n\nThe **Dubai to Abu Dhabi bus ticket price** is AED 25 per journey. Payment requires a Nol card, the same smart card used throughout Dubai's public transportation system.\n\n**Nol card requirements:**\n\n- Minimum load: AED 25 for a one-way journey\n\n- Recommended load: AED 50 for a round trip\n\n- Available at: Bus stations, metro stations, selected retail outlets\n\n\n### Bus comfort and amenities\n\nMost buses feature:\n\n- Air conditioning with individual controls\n\n- Reclining seats with ample legroom\n\n- USB charging ports and power outlets\n\n- Free Wi-Fi (limited coverage)\n\n- Under-bus luggage storage\n\n- Priority seating arrangements\n\n\nThe journey offers scenic views of the UAE's desert landscape and developing urban areas. Many travellers use this time to plan their Abu Dhabi itinerary or catch up on work.\n\n## Metro Plus Bus Combination from Dubai to Abu Dhabi\n\nWhile there's no direct **Dubai to Abu Dhabi distance by train**, a combination route exists using Dubai Metro and intercity buses.\n\n### Journey breakdown\n\n**Part 1: Dubai Metro (Red Line)**\n\n- Route: Any station to Ibn Battuta Mall\n\n- Travel Time: Varies by starting point (typically 30-60 minutes)\n\n- Cost: AED 3-8 depending on zones\n\n\n**Part 2: Transfer to the bus**\n\n- Walking distance: 5-10 minutes from the metro to the bus stop\n\n- Waiting time: Up to 20 minutes for the next bus\n\n\n**Part 3: Bus to Abu Dhabi**\n\n- Route: E101 from Ibn Battuta to Abu Dhabi Central\n\n- Travel Time: 1 hour 30 minutes\n\n- Cost: AED 25\n\n\n### When to choose this option\n\nThis combination works best for travellers already using the Dubai Metro or staying near metro stations. However, the total journey time (2.5-3 hours) makes it less attractive for time-conscious travellers.\n\n## Why There's No Direct Train or Flight from Dubai to Abu Dhabi\n\nMany visitors wonder about train or flight options for this route. Here's why these aren't available:\n\n### Railway infrastructure\n\nThe UAE lacks intercity passenger rail service. While freight rail exists, passenger rail development focuses on longer routes like the planned UAE-Saudi Arabia connection.\n\n### Aviation logistics\n\nThe short **distance between Dubai and Abu Dhabi** makes flights impractical. Airport procedures (check-in, security, boarding) would take longer than the actual travel time between cities.\n\n## Cost Comparison and Travel Times from Dubai to Abu Dhabi\n\nUnderstanding total costs helps you make informed decisions based on your travel style and budget:\n\n| Transport mode | Cost (one-way) | Travel time | Convenience level |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| Private Car/Taxi | AED 250-400 | 1hr 22min | High |\n| Bus (Direct) | AED 25 | 1hr 40min | Medium |\n| Metro + Bus | AED 28-33 | 2hr 30min | Low |\n| Car Rental | AED 100-150/day + fuel | 1hr 22min | High |\n\n### Hidden costs to consider\n\n**Parking in Abu Dhabi:** AED 2-5 per hour in the city centre\n\n**Toll roads:** Included in taxi fares; minimal for private cars\n\n**Return journey:** Double all costs for round-trip trips\n\nFor multiple days in Abu Dhabi, consider accommodation near major attractions. This reduces daily transportation needs and allows more time for experiences like [Yas Island attractions](https://alike.io/products/yas-island-multipark-ticket).\n\n## Best Transportation Option from Dubai to Abu Dhabi as per Your Needs\n\nChoosing the right transportation depends on your priorities, budget, and travel style:\n\n### Choose a private car/taxi if:\n\n- You value time over cost\n\n- Travelling with family or large groups\n\n- Planning multiple stops along the route\n\n- Carrying significant luggage\n\n- Prefer door-to-door convenience\n\n\n### Choose the bus if:\n\n- Budget is your primary concern\n\n- You enjoy meeting other travellers\n\n- Environmental impact matters to you\n\n- You don't mind slightly longer journey times\n\n- Travelling solo or as a couple\n\n\n### Choose the metro + bus option if:\n\n- You're already staying near metro stations\n\n- You want to experience Dubai's public transport\n\n- Cost savings are crucial\n\n- You have flexible time schedules\n\n\n### Additional considerations for families\n\nFamilies with young children often prefer private transportation for convenience and comfort. However, buses offer entertainment value for children who enjoy watching the landscape change. Plan bathroom breaks accordingly, as bus stops are limited.\n\n## Making Your Journey Seamless\n\nRegardless of your chosen transportation method, these tips ensure smooth travel:\n\n### Advance planning:\n\n- Check traffic conditions before departure\n\n- Download offline maps for backup navigation\n\n- Inform your accommodation about arrival times\n\n\n### Essential items:\n\n- Valid identification (required for hotel check-ins)\n\n- Phone chargers and power banks\n\n- Light jacket (air conditioning can be cold)\n\n- Snacks and water for longer journeys\n\n\n### Cultural considerations:\n\n- Dress modestly, especially when visiting religious sites\n\n- Learn basic Arabic greetings\n\n- Respect local customs and traditions\n\n\n### Weather impact on travel\n\nUAE's climate affects journey comfort. Summer months (June-September) make air-conditioned transportation essential. Winter months (November-March) offer pleasant weather for road trips with windows down.\n\n**Ready to plan your UAE adventure?** Start with our [AI Trip Planner](https://alike.io/trip-planner) to create personalised itineraries combining the best of both cities. For hassle-free attraction access, check out our [Dubai Tourist Pass](https://alike.io/dubai-tourist-pass) and [Abu Dhabi city experiences](https://alike.io/cities/abu-dhabi) to make every moment count during your Emirates journey.\n\n_Disclaimer: All prices mentioned are approximate and subject to change._\n\n## Key Takeaways\n\n- **Dubai to Abu Dhabi distance** by road is 140 kilometres, taking 1 hour 22 minutes by car under normal traffic conditions.\n\n- Bus travel costs just **AED 25** compared to **AED 250-400** for taxis, making it the most budget-friendly option.\n\n- Private car or taxi offers the best convenience and time efficiency for families and business travellers.\n\n- Avoid peak hours (7-9 AM and 5-7 PM) to prevent journey times extending to nearly 2 hours.\n\n- No advance booking required for buses — they run every 20 minutes between major stations.\n\n- **Abu Dhabi to Dubai metro fare** doesn't exist as direct rail service isn't available between the cities.\n\n\nFrequently Asked Questions\n\nHow long does it take to travel from Dubai to Abu Dhabi?\n\nThe fastest option is a private car or taxi at 1 hour 22 minutes. Bus travel takes approximately 1 hour and 40 minutes.\n\nWhat is the cheapest way to get from Dubai to Abu Dhabi?\n\nPublic bus service at AED 25 per journey offers the most economical option.\n\nIs there a train from Dubai to Abu Dhabi?\n\nNo direct train service exists. A metro-plus-bus combination is available but takes significantly longer.\n\nDo I need a visa to travel between Dubai and Abu Dhabi?\n\nNo, both cities are within the UAE. Valid identification is sufficient for intercity travel.\n\nWhat is the distance between Dubai and Abu Dhabi?\n\nThe road distance is 140 kilometres, while the aerial distance is 123 kilometres.\n\nCan I book bus tickets in advance for Dubai to Abu Dhabi?\n\nNo advance booking is required or available for intercity buses. Simply arrive at the station with a loaded Nol card and board the next available bus. They depart every 20 minutes throughout the day.\n\nTable of Contents\n\nMore From Author\n\nRelated Learn flat AED 2 city fares, Hafilat card types, and terminal departure hours.\n\nFare StructureFlat AED 2.00 city (AED 0.05/km reg)\n\nSmart CardHafilat Card (Anonymous & Personalized)\n\nRoutes CoveredRoute 5, 7, 8, 9, 11, 24, 34, 40, 54, 56...\n\nKey HubsCentral Bus Station, Marina Mall, Zayed Port\n\n[Browse Abu Dhabi Bus Routes (20+) →](https://dxbnavigator.com/abu-dhabi-bus-routes/)\n\nSharjah · SRTA Mowasalat🕌\n\n## Dubai Metro Network & Practical Travel Guides\nUnderstand fare tiers across 7 pricing zones, Nol card types, transfer rules, and operating hours.\n\nRed Line · 35 Stations🔴\n\n### Dubai Metro Red Line Guide\nConnecting Centrepoint (Rashidiya) near DXB Terminal 3 to Expo City Dubai and UAE Exchange (Jebel Ali). High-capacity spine through Downtown, DIFC, and Dubai Marina.\n\nOperating Hours05:00 AM to 12:00 Midnight\n\nAirport TerminalsDirect DXB Terminals 1 & 3 links\n\nInterchange StationsUnion & BurJuman (Green Line)\n\nExpress FaresAED 3.00 to AED 7.50 Silver Nol\n\n[Explore Red Line Stations →](https://dxbnavigator.com/intercity-bus/)\n\nGreen Line · 20 Stations🟢\n\n### Dubai Nol Card Guide & Rules\nChoosing between Silver (frequent riders), Gold (VIP carriages), Red Ticket (tourist single trips), and Blue (personalized student & senior citizen concessions).\n\nSilver CardAED 25 purchase (AED 19 e-purse)\n\nGold Carriage2x Silver fare rate\n\nFree TransfersWithin 30 mins across 3 modes\n\nIntercity ValidityAccepted on all Dubai RTA buses\n\n[Learn Nol Card Rules →](https://dxbnavigator.com/intercity-bus/)\n\n🛡️ INDEPENDENT & VERIFIED DATA\n\n## Frequently Asked Questions About UAE Transit & Living\nQuick answers to essential questions about public transport, utility costs, business licenses, visas, and traffic fines in the UAE.\n\nWhich smart travel cards are required across the different Emirates?\n\nPublic transit smart cards in the UAE are managed by each Emirate's individual transport authority: Dubai uses the **Nol Card** (valid on Metro, Tram, city buses, water buses, and Dubai-operated intercity buses); Abu Dhabi uses the **Hafilat Smart Card** (valid on all city, regional, and Al Ain buses); Sharjah uses the **Sayer Card** (valid on SRTA city buses); and Ajman uses the **Masaar Card**. For intercity buses departing from Dubai, you pay using your Nol Card at the terminal gates.\n\nWhy are minimum taxi fares different in Dubai, Sharjah, and Abu Dhabi?\n\nEach Emirate's transport authority sets its own statutory fare regulations. In Dubai, the RTA enforces a minimum fare of **AED 12.00**. In Abu Dhabi, the Integrated Transport Centre (ITC) enforces an **AED 12.00** minimum. In Sharjah, the Roads & Transport Authority (SRTA) enforces an **AED 16.50** minimum fare floor. When a taxi crosses between Emirates (e.g. Sharjah to Dubai), an official **AED 20.00 intercity cross-border surcharge** is automatically applied to the meter.\n\nHow is the housing fee calculated on DEWA and ADDC utility bills?\n\nIn Dubai, the Dubai Municipality housing fee for expatriates is equal to **5% of the annual tenancy contract value** (as registered in Ejari), divided into 12 equal monthly installments on your DEWA bill. In Abu Dhabi, the municipality fee (Tawtheeq) is also **5% of the annual rent** divided by 12, with a statutory minimum fee of AED 450 per year (AED 37.50 per month). In Sharjah, residential tenants pay a 1.5 fils per gallon sewerage fee and tenancy municipality charges.\n\nHow much does the Etihad Rail passenger train cost?\n\nOfficial Etihad Rail passenger fares start at **AED 39.00** for a standard Comfort Saver ticket between Abu Dhabi Station and Dubai Station (105 km in 64 minutes) and **AED 109.00** for Premium Class. Children aged 2 to 17 receive a **50% discount** (AED 20 for Abu Dhabi ⇄ Dubai Comfort), and infants under 2 travel free on lap. Optional Value (+AED 20) and Flex (+AED 40) tiers provide seat selection and refundable ticket flexibility.\n\nHow accurate are the UAE Business License Cost Calculators?\n\nOur calculators provide benchmark estimates within 90%+ accuracy based on 2026 published mainland and free zone fee schedules across Dubai (DET, IFZA, Meydan, DMCC), Sharjah (SEDD, SHAMS, SPC), Ajman (AFZA, AMCFZ), and Ras Al Khaimah (RAKEZ). Actual final quotes may vary slightly based on specific commercial activities, immigration security approvals, and chosen office configurations.\n\nHow does the UAE Visa Cost Calculator factor in mandatory health insurance?\n\nIn the UAE, health insurance is legally mandatory for all residency visa holders. In Dubai (under DHA) and federally across the Northern Emirates, sponsors must provide minimum Essential Benefits Plan (EBP) coverage (approx. AED 800/year for dependents) or comprehensive coverage (approx. AED 2,200+/year). Our calculator includes these insurance tiers alongside government entry permits, status change fees, medical fitness screening, and Emirates ID.\n\nDo traffic fine discounts apply automatically in Sharjah and Ajman?\n\nYes. In Sharjah, eligible traffic fines paid within 60 days of the violation date automatically receive a 35% discount, or a 25% discount if paid between 61 days and 365 days. In Ajman, a 50% discount window applies to eligible violations. However, serious offenses—such as jumping a red light, exceeding the speed limit by over 80 km/h, and reckless driving endangering lives—are statutory exceptions and never receive early payment discounts.\n\nHow can I travel between Dubai and Abu Dhabi by public transit?\n\nYou can take express intercity buses operated by RTA and ITC: **Route E100** departs from Al Ghubaiba Bus Station in Bur Dubai to Abu Dhabi Central Bus Station, and **Route E101** departs from Ibn Battuta Bus Station to Abu Dhabi Central. Both cost **AED 25.00** one-way paid with a standard Nol Card, departing every 15 to 20 minutes throughout the day.",

Dhabi uses the **Hafilat Smart Card** (valid on all city, regional, and Al Ain buses); Sharjah uses the **Sayer Card** (valid on SRTA city buses); and Ajman uses the **Masaar Card**. For intercity buses departing from Dubai, you pay using your Nol Card at the terminal gates.\n\nWhy are minimum taxi fares different in Dubai, Sharjah, and Abu Dhabi?\n\nEach Emirate's transport authority sets its own statutory fare regulations. In Dubai, the RTA enforces a minimum fare of **AED 12.00**. In Abu Dhabi, the Integrated Transport Centre (ITC) enforces an **AED 12.00** minimum. In Sharjah, the Roads & Transport Authority (SRTA) enforces an **AED 16.50** minimum fare floor. When a taxi crosses between Emirates (e.g. Sharjah to Dubai), an official **AED 20.00 intercity cross-border surcharge** is automatically applied to the meter.\n\nHow is the housing fee calculated on DEWA and ADDC utility bills?\n\nIn Dubai, the Dubai Municipality housing fee for expatriates is equal to **5% of the annual tenancy contract value** (as registered in Ejari), divided into 12 equal monthly installments on your DEWA bill. In Abu Dhabi, the municipality fee (Tawtheeq) is also **5% of the annual rent** divided by 12, with a statutory minimum fee of AED 450 per year (AED 37.50 per month). In Sharjah, residential tenants pay a 1.5 fils per gallon sewerage fee and tenancy municipality charges.\n\nHow much does the Etihad Rail passenger train cost?\n\nOfficial Etihad Rail passenger fares start at **AED 39.00** for a standard Comfort Saver ticket between Abu Dhabi Station and Dubai Station (105 km in 64 minutes) and **AED 109.00** for Premium Class. Children aged 2 to 17 receive a **50% discount** (AED 20 for Abu Dhabi ⇄ Dubai Comfort), and infants under 2 travel free on lap. Optional Value (+AED 20) and Flex (+AED 40) tiers provide seat selection and refundable ticket flexibility.\n\nHow accurate are the UAE Business License Cost Calculators?\n\nOur calculators provide benchmark estimates within 90%+ accuracy based on 2026 published mainland and free zone fee schedules across Dubai (DET, IFZA, Meydan, DMCC), Sharjah (SEDD, SHAMS, SPC), Ajman (AFZA, AMCFZ), and Ras Al Khaimah (RAKEZ). Actual final quotes may vary slightly based on specific commercial activities, immigration security approvals, and chosen office configurations.\n\nHow does the UAE Visa Cost Calculator factor in mandatory health insurance?\n\nIn the UAE, health insurance is legally mandatory for all residency visa holders. In Dubai (under DHA) and federally across the Northern Emirates, sponsors must provide minimum Essential Benefits Plan (EBP) coverage (approx. AED 800/year for dependents) or comprehensive coverage (approx. AED 2,200+/year). Our calculator includes these insurance tiers alongside government entry permits, status change fees, medical fitness screening, and Emirates ID.\n\nDo traffic fine discounts apply automatically in Sharjah and Ajman?\n\nYes. In Sharjah, eligible traffic fines paid within 60 days of the violation date automatically receive a 35% discount, or a 25% discount if paid between 61 days and 365 days. In Ajman, a 50% discount window applies to eligible violations. However, serious offenses—such as jumping a red light, exceeding the speed limit by over 80 km/h, and reckless driving endangering lives—are statutory exceptions and never receive early payment discounts.\n\nHow can I travel between Dubai and Abu Dhabi by public transit?\n\nYou can take express intercity buses operated by RTA and ITC: **Route E100** departs from Al Ghubaiba Bus Station in Bur Dubai to Abu Dhabi Central Bus Station, and **Route E101** departs from Ibn Battuta Bus Station to Abu Dhabi Central. Both cost **AED 25.00** one-way paid with a standard Nol Card, departing every 15 to 20 minutes throughout the day.",
